NATIONAL AFFAIRS • CABINET APPROVALS
1.1  Cabinet Approves Expanded Khelo India Scheme and Enhanced ANSFs with Rs.36,441 Crore Outlayp.6
Exam Hints:
  • What: Approval of expanded Khelo India Scheme and enhanced ANSF scheme
  • Approved by: Union Cabinet chaired by PM Narendra Modi
  • Outlay: Rs.36,441 crore (Khelo India: Rs. 29,054 crore; ANSF: Rs. 7,387 crore)
  • Implementation Period: FY27 to FY31
  • Alignment: Khelo Bharat Niti 2025, NEP 2020
  • New Initiatives: Khelo India Feeder Schools & Khelo India Utkrishta Vidyalayas
  • New Category: Emerging Khelo India Athletes
  • Olympic Pathway: Linked to Target Olympic Podium Scheme (TOPS)
  • Enhanced Assistance to National Sports Federations Scheme: Objective: Strengthen NSFs and support Indian athletesSupport Includes: Coaching, sports science, modern equipment, training camps, ets.
Background:
  • Government has allocated Rs. 29,054 crore for the Khelo India Scheme and Rs. 7,387 crore for the ANSF Scheme over the next five years, with the total outlay being nearly eight times that of the previous Khelo India Scheme (FY17–FY26).
About Khelo India Scheme:
  • Background: Launched in 2017–18, the Khelo India Scheme was created by integrating three erstwhile schemes: Rajiv Gandhi Khel Abhiyan (RGKA) launched in 2014, Urban Sports Infrastructure Scheme (USIS) launched in 2010, and National Sports Talent Search Scheme (NSTSS) launched in 2015 to create a unified framework for sports development in India
  • Khelo India School Games: The inaugural edition was held in January 2018 in New Delhi, Delhi and was later renamed the Khelo India Youth Games (KIYG) in 2019 to include university and college-level athletes.
Assistance to National Sports Federations (ANSF) Scheme:
  • Approval: Government has approved a strengthened ANSF Scheme for the 2026–31 period to enhance support for Indian sports.
Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports (MYAS) - Static GK:
Union Minister: Dr. Mansukh Mandaviya (Constituency – Porbandar, Gujarat)
Minister of State (MoS): Raksha Khadse (Constituency – Raver, Maharashtra)

NATIONAL AFFAIRS • CABINET APPROVALS
1.2  Cabinet Approves Rs 84,084 crore ‘Samudra Manthan’ Scheme to boost Offshore oil and Gas Explorationp.8
Exam Hints:
  • What: Approval of ‘Samudra Manthan’-NOE scheme
  • Approved by: Union Cabinet, chaired by PM Narendra Modi
  • Total Budget: Rs 84,084 crore
  • Scheme Duration: FY31
  • Nature of Scheme: CSS (100% funding by the central government)
  • Key Targets: Domestic oil and gas production: 80 MMTOE (annually);Increase Hydrocarbon Resource Base: 2,200 MMTOE (annually)Expected Outcome: Accelerate reserve accretion of over 600 MMTOE
Background:
  • As per the Government of India (GoI), the newly approved scheme will be implemented through Financial Year 2030-31 (FY31).
About Samudra Manthan Scheme:
  • Type of Scheme: Central Sector Scheme (CSS) (100% funding by the central government) of the Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas (MoPNG).
  • Funding Allocation: Of the total budget approved for the scheme,Rs 43,200 crore allocated for the support of deepwater drilling activities over the next 5 years.Rs 28,534 crore for offshore data acquisition.Rs 10,000 earmarked for common production and evacuation infrastructure.Remaining Rs 2,000 crore will be used for the establishment of oil and gas manufacturing and services hubs.
  • Significance: The new scheme is expected to accelerate reserve accretion of over 600 Million Metric Tonnes of Oil Equivalent gas (MMTOE).
Ministry of Petroleum & Natural Gas (MoP&NG) - Static GK:
Union Minister: Hardeep Singh Puri (Rajya Sabha Member- Uttar Pradesh, UP)
Union Minister of State (MoS): Suresh Gopi (Constituency- Thrissur, Kerala)

NATIONAL AFFAIRS • CABINET APPROVALS
1.3  Cabinet Approves Rs 5,070 Crore to PM-SSY for Floating Solar Projectsp.9
Exam Hints:
  • What scheme: Pradhan Mantri Surya Sarovar Yojana (PM-SSY)
  • Which Ministry: Under MNRE
  • Approved By: Union Cabinet
  • Approval Year: 2026
  • Outlay: Rs 5,070 crore
  • Objective: FSPV projects with ESS
  • Target Capacity: 5,000 MW Floating Solar Projects
  • Sanction Period: FY 2026-27 to FY 2030-31
Background:
  • The scheme aims to develop 5,000 Megawatt (MW)of floating solar capacity with co-located ESS to strengthen renewable energy generation and grid reliability.
About PM-SSY for Floating Solar projects:
  • Features: The projects will be sanctioned during FY 2026-27 to FY 2030-31, with financial support continuing up to FY 2032-33, and will include ESS with a minimum storage capacity of 2 hours i.e. 10,000 MWh (megawatt-hour)
  • Potential: National Institute of Solar Energy (NISE) estimated India’s floating solar potential at 102.18 Gigawatt Peak (GWp) across reservoirs and inland water bodies, while the scheme aims to increase the current floating solar capacity of around 700 MW.
  • Impact: The scheme is expected to reduce around 10 million tonnes of CO₂ emissions annually and generate 16,000–17,000 employment opportunities across the project value chain.The initiative will promote domestic manufacturing of floating systems, photovoltaic cells, modules and energy storage systems, supporting Aatmanirbhar Bharat and Viksit Bharat.
Ministry of New and Renewable Energy (MNRE) - Static GK:
Union Minister: Pralhad Joshi (Constituency – Dharwad, Karnataka)
Minister of State (MoS): Sripad Naik (Constituency – North Goa)

NATIONAL AFFAIRS • CABINET APPROVALS
1.4  Cabinet Approves PM-KISAN Scheme for Next Five Years with Rs. 3.15 Lakh Crorep.10
Exam Hints:
  • What: Approval for Continuation of PM-KISAN Scheme
  • Approved by: Union Cabinet
  • Extended Timeline: 5 years (from 2026-27 to 2030-31)
  • Total Budget: Rs 3.15 lakhcrore
  • Annual Financial Support: Rs 6,000(3 equal installments of Rs 2,000 each)
  • Total Assistance Released: Over Rs. 4.47 lakh crore (23 installments).
About Pradhan Mantri Kisan Samman Nidhi (PM-KISAN):
  • Background: Launched in February 2019, PM-KISAN, is a Central Sector Scheme (CSS) i.e 100% funding by the central government.
  • Budget for FY27: GoI allocated Rs 60,000 crore for the PM-KISAN scheme in the Union Budget for Financial Year 2026-27 (FY27).
Key Achievements:
  • Total Amount Disbursed: So far, the GoI has transferred over Rs 4.47 lakh crore directly into the bank accounts of farmers through 23 installments.In June 2026, during his visit to West Bengal (WB), PM Narendra Modi released the 23rd instalment of the scheme worth over Rs 18,880 crore, benefitting more than 9.49 crore farmers (including over 2.18 crore women farmers) across the country.During the COVID-19 pandemic, over Rs 1.71 lakh crore was disbursed to support farmers.
  • Growth in Cultivation and Productivity: Between 2020-21 and 2025-26, the area under cultivation expanded by nearly 9.6%, agricultural productivity increased by around 10.53%, and total foodgrain production recorded growth of about 21.18%.

1.5  Cabinet Approves Rs 23,731 Crore GOBARdhan Scheme; Rs 8,970.20 crore NH Project in Assamp.11
Exam Hints:
  • What: Approval of GOBARdhan scheme
  • Approved by: Union Cabinet, chaired by PM Narendra Modi
  • Total Budget Outlay: Rs 23,731 crore
  • Implementation Period: 10 years (from FY27 to FY36)
  • Implementing Ministry/Agency: MoPNG
  • Key Target: Increase domestic CBG production by nearly 10-times
  • Key Components of Scheme: Assured CBG Offtake;Stable CBG pricing framework (Rs 2,110 per MMBTU);Capital assistance (up to Rs 2 crore per TPD);Development of Pipeline Infrastructure;Credit Guarantee Support;CBG Ecosystem Challenge FundOther Key Approval: 4-lane NH project in AssamApproved by: CCEA, chaired by PM ModiTotal Cost: Rs 8,970.20 croreTotal Length: 135.87 kmExecution Mode: BOT Toll model as part of NH(O) programme
Background:
  • The scheme will be implemented by the Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas (MoPNG) for a period of 10 years i.e. from FY27 to FY36.
Key Components of Scheme:
  • Assured CBG Offtake: The scheme introduces a dedicated CBG Offtake Assurance framework to provide a reliable and predictable market for producers.Under the offtake assurance framework, City Gas Distribution (CGD) entities will procure the fuel to meet the GoI’s mandatory blending targets of 3% (FY27), 4% (FY28) and 5% from (FY29) onwards in the CNG (Transport) and PNG (Domestic) segments.
CCEA Approved Rs 8,970 crore NH Project in Assam:
  • Approved by: Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs (CCEA), chaired by PM Narendra Modi, approved the construction of a 135.87-kilometer (km)-long four-lane access-controlled Guwahati–Tezpur corridor on National Highway (NH-15) in Assam.
  • Development Mode: The newly approved NH project will be developed under the Build-Operate-Transfer (BOT) Toll model as part of the NH (Original) NH (O)programme, at an estimated cost of Rs 8,970.20 crore.
Ministry of Petroleum & Natural Gas (MoP&NG) - Static GK:
Union Minister: Hardeep Singh Puri (Rajya Sabha Member- Uttar Pradesh, UP)
Union Minister of State (MoS): Suresh Gopi (Constituency- Thrissur, Kerala)

1.6  Cabinet Approved Railways and Highway Projects worth Rs 13,041 Crore across 5 Statesp.12
Exam Hints:
  • What: Approval of 5 key Infrastructure Projects
  • Approved by: Union Cabinet, chaired by PM Narendra Modi
  • Total Cost: Rs 13,041 crore
  • Approval-1: 4 Multi-tracking Projects
  • Estimated Cost: Rs 9,450 crore
  • Coverage: 8 Districts of 4 States (WB, TN, AP and Odisha)
  • Approval-2: 4-laning of NH-22 in Bihar
  • Total Cost: Rs 3,590.73 crore
  • Length: 82.578 km
  • Execution Mode: HAM
About Multi-tracking Projects:
  • Districts-wise Coverage: The proposed projects include 173 -km long 4th line between Kharagpur and Bhadrak (Ranital) in WB and Odisha (with estimated cost of around Rs 3,352 crore); the 75-km 4th line between Bhadrak and Haridaspur in Odisha (at a cost of nearly Rs 1,583 crore); the 90 km 3rd and 4th lines between Gummidipundi-Gudur across AP and TN (Rs 2,229 crore); and 72-km 3rd and 4th lines between Cuttack and Paradeep (Badabandha) in Odisha (2,286 crore).
Upgradation of NH-22 in Bihar:
  • Approval: CCEA approved the upgradation of the 82.578-km Muzaffarpur-Sitamarhi-Sonbarsa section of NH-22 to four-lane standard in Bihar.
  • Key Provisions: The approved project includes provisions for 07 major bridges, featuring a 340-metre (m)-long bridge over the perennial Bagmati River, 03 Railway OverBridges (ROBs) and 02 flyovers measuring 1,170 m and 270 m.
  • Aligns with PMGS-NMP: The newly approved project planned in accordance with the PMGS-NMP.
About Bihar:
  • Chief Minister (CM)- Samrat Choudhary
  • Capital: Patna National Parks (NP)- Valmiki NP

1.7  Union Cabinet Approves Permanent Bench of J&K High Court in Ladakhp.14
Exam Hints:
  • What: Approval of permanent bench of High Court of J&K and Ladakh in Ladakh
  • Approved By: Union Cabinet
  • High Court: HC of Jammu & Kashmir and Ladakh
  • Location: Ladakh (3rd judicial seat)
  • Objective: Improve access to justice in remote regions.
About J&K–Ladakh High Court Bench:
  • Structure: HC of J&K and Ladakh is the common HC for the UT of J&K and Ladakh.
  • Benefits: Ladakh, a high-altitude UT with scattered settlements across Leh and Kargil districts, will benefit from the new bench as it reduces the need for litigants to travel to Jammu or Srinagar for court hearings.
  • Significance: The permanent bench will enhance ease of legal access for people of Ladakh.

NATIONAL AFFAIRS • LAUNCHES & INAUGURATION
1.8  DFS Launches Sovereign-Backed P&I Insurance Product under BMIPp.14
Exam Hints:
  • What: Launch of India’s first sovereign-backed P&I Insurance
  • By Whom: DFS
  • Designed by: NIACL
  • Hand Over: To SCI
  • Key Features: USD 1.5 bn cover; crew, cargo, pollution & wreck removal liabilities; 24×7 port network.
  • Objective: Reduce foreign reliance, strengthen India’s maritime insurance ecosystem.
About P&I Insurance Product:
  • Framework: BMIP became operational on 12 May 2026 with a sovereign guarantee of Rs.12,980 crore (approximately USD 1.5 billion) to provide uninterrupted marine insurance capacity for Indian shipping against war-related and other maritime risks.
About Bharat Maritime Insurance Pool (BMIP):
  • Overview: BMIP is India’s first sovereign-backed maritime insurance framework, launched in two phases with the War Risk framework on 12 May 2026 and the P&I product on 30 July 2026 to protect maritime trade from geopolitical shocks, sanctions, and volatile insurance premiums.
  • Finance: BMIP has a financial capacity of Rs.13,906 crore (approximately USD 1.5 billion), comprising a Rs.12,980 crore sovereign guarantee, Rs.926 crore seed capital from 23 domestic insurers, a USD 100 million government backstop trigger threshold, and an initial 10-year tenure extendable by five years.
The New India Assurance Company Limited (NIACL) - Static GK:
Chairman and Managing Director (CMD): Girija Subramanian
Headquarters: Mumbai, Maharashtra
Established: 1919

1.9  Union Minister Giriraj Singh Launches ‘Kapas Darshan’ Bulletin, Four MoUs Signed at CCI’s 56th Foundation Dayp.16
Exam Hints:
  • What: 56th Foundation Day of CCI celebrated
  • Where: Chhatrapati Sambhajinagar, Maharashtra
  • Launch: Kapas Darshan Bulletin launched
  • Launched by: Union Minister Giriraj Singh, MoT
  • MoUs: Four MoUs signed – CCI-ICAR-CIRCOT, CCI-GIZ, CITI-ICAC-MERAGO/GEOCLARA, TEXPROCIL-Manjeet Cotton
About Four MoUs Signed:
  • Traceability: Cotton Textiles Export Promotion Council (TEXPROCIL) and Manjeet Cotton Private Limited signed a MoU to collaborate under the Kasturi Cotton Bharat (KCB) programme for the production and certification of 30,000 cotton bales (20,000 Long Staple and 10,000 Extra Long Staple), ensuring traceability, compliance with quality standards, and the development of a premium, traceable cotton supply chain.
About the Cotton Productivity Mission – Kapas Kanti:
  • Overview: Launched in May 2026, the Cotton Productivity Mission – Kapas Kanti has an outlay of Rs.5,659 crore over five years to improve cotton productivity, fibre quality, farmer income, sustainable cultivation, processing, traceability, branding and global competitiveness.
  • Procurement: During Cotton Season 2025–26, CCI procured over 522 lakh quintals of seed cotton through nearly 24 lakh farmer transactions and transferred Rs.41,530 crore directly into Aadhaar-linked bank accounts.
Cotton Corporation of India Limited (CCI) - Static GK:
Chairman & Managing Director (CMD): Lalit Kumar Gupta
Headquarters: Mumbai, Maharashtra
Established: 1970

1.10  PM Modi launched 100-week long ‘Nasha Mukt Yuva for Viksit Bharat Sankalp Abhiyan’p.17
Exam Hints:
  • What: Launch of Nasha Mukht Yuva for Viksit Bharat Sankalp Abhiyan
  • Launched by: PM Narendra Modi
  • Key Objectives: To mobilise young people against substance abuse and build drug-free India
  • Duration: 100 week
Background:
  • Through a nationwide video conference, PM Modi addressed over 1 crore youth connected from over 28,000 locations, and announced that the campaign would run every Sunday for the next 100 weeks with the objective to make drug-free India.
GoI-led Initiatives:
  • Nasha Mukt Bharat Abhiyan (NMBA): It is a flagship initiative of the Ministry of Social Justice & Empowerment (MoSJE), launched in August 2020 under the National Action Plan for Drug Demand Reduction (NAPDDR).Till 2025, more than 23.74 crore persons sensitised, including 8.09 crore youth and 5.49 crore women, under the NMBA.
  • Nasha Mukt Bharat Saptah: Department of Social Justice & Empowerment (DoSJE) celebrated the “International Day Against Drug Abuse and Illicit Trafficking (observed annually on June 206)” organised “Nasha Mukt Bharat Saptah” from June 17 to June 26, 2026 across the country under the theme “Nasha Mukt Bharat Abhiyaan- Viksit Bharat Ki Pehchaan”.

1.11  DoP Releases Commemorative Postage Stamp to Mark Birth Centenary of Padma Vibhushan Satish Gujralp.18
Exam Hints:
  • What: Dop released Rs.5 Commemorative Postage Stamp
  • Occasion: 100th birth anniversary of Satish Gujral (1925–2020)
  • Unveiled by: Union Minister Jyotiraditya M. Scindia, Ministry of Communications
  • Designed by: Anuj Kumar
  • Award: Padma Vibhushan in 1999.
Background:
  • The stamp was unveiled by Union Minister Jyotiraditya M. Scindia, Ministry of Communications, to honour his outstanding contribution to Indian art, architecture and culture.
About Commemorative Postage Stamp:
  • Design: The commemorative postage stamp was designed by Anuj Kumar based on a proposal by Raseel Gujral Ansal.
  • Artwork: It features Satish Gujral’s 2010 acrylic self-portrait with a black-and-white photo-realistic finish set against a vibrant purple-and-blue background.
  • About Satish Gujral: He was one of India’s foremost multidisciplinary artists, internationally recognised for his contributions as a painter, sculptor, architect, muralist, and writer over a career spanning more than seven decades.

NATIONAL AFFAIRS • LAUNCHES & INAUGURATION
1.12  IIT Hyderabad and Crimson Energy Launch India’s First Nuclear Training Programme ‘ANUGYAN’p.18
Exam Hints:
  • What: Launch of India’s first nuclear training programme
  • Programme: ANUGYAN
  • Launched By: IIT Hyderabad and CEEPL
  • Duration: 3-month residential programme
  • Objective: Develop skilled professionals for India’s nuclear energy ecosystem
  • Focus Areas: Nuclear technology, reactor systems, safety, regulations, and project management.
About ANUGYAN Nuclear Training Programme:
  • Objective: three-month residential programme aims to bridge the talent gap by developing skilled nuclear professionals to support India’s goal of expanding its nuclear power capacity from around 8.8 GW (Giga Watt) to 100 GW by 2047.
  • Eligibility: The programme is open to engineering graduates, working professionals, Public Sector Undertaking (PSU) executives, Engineering, Procurement and Construction (EPC) contractors, and industrial equipment manufacturers.
  • Curriculum: The programme covers key areas of nuclear engineering, including reactor physics, thermal hydraulics, radiation shielding, Small Modular Reactors (SMRs), engineering design, nuclear safety, quality assurance, Atomic Energy Regulatory Board (AERB) regulatory compliance, and simulator-based training.

Background:

About Indian Institute of Technology Delhi (IIT-D):

1.14  Union Minister Shivraj Singh Chouhan Launches NSP-Based Scholarship & Fellowship Disbursement Initiative for Agriculture Studentsp.20
Exam Hints:
  • What: Launch of NSP-based scholarship and fellowship disbursement system for agriculture students
  • Who: Union Minister Shivraj Singh Chouhan, MoAFW
  • Total Value of Funds: Rs 21.35 crore
  • Mode of Funds Release: NSP-DBT
  • Scholarship Frequency: Monthly
Background:
  • Under the initiative, Rs 21.35 crore was disbursed through Direct Benefit Transfer (DBT) directly to the bank accounts of more than 6,000 students.
Key Details of NSP Initiative:
  • Aim: To make the scholarship disbursement process more streamlined and ensure hassle-free manner financial assistance.
  • Disbursement Mechanism: Under the newly launched initiative, agriculture students will now receive their scholarship amounts every month through DBT without facing unnecessary difficulties or delays.
  • Key Focus: The new initiative will focus on integrating agricultural education with technology, practical training and industry orientation to prepare students for emerging challenges in the agriculture sector.
  • ICAR-IARI: Established as the Agricultural Research Institute (ARI) in Pusa, Bihar, in 1905, it was shifted to Delhi in 1936 and renamed IARI after Independence.

1.15  NABL Launches India’s First Accreditation Scheme for Mobile Food Testing Laboratoriesp.20
Exam Hints:
  • What: NABL launched India’s first accreditation scheme for Mobile Food Testing Laboratories.
  • Launched by: NABL
  • Parent Organisation: QCI
  • Event: Gunvatta Samanvaya – A Multilateral MoU Exchange Ceremony
  • Venue: Hotel Ashoka, New Delhi, Delhi
  • Framework: IAP
Background:
  • The scheme was launched during the “Gunvatta Samanvaya – A Multilateral Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) Exchange Ceremony” held at Hotel Ashoka, New Delhi, Delhi.
Key Highlights of the India’s First Accreditation Scheme:
  • Dignitaries: The event was attended by Amardeep Singh Bhatia, Secretary of DPIIT; Dr Sandip Shah, Chairperson of NABL; and Chakravarthy T. Kannan, Secretary General of QCI.
  • Testing: The scheme enables mobile food testing laboratories to provide reliable, quality-assured and internationally benchmarked testing services closer to communities.
  • Food Safety: The initiative strengthens food safety surveillance, improves access to testing facilities, enables faster regulatory action and enhances consumer confidence.
  • Framework: The scheme under NABL’s Integrated Assessment Programme (IAP) promotes efficient assessments, mutual recognition and regulatory convergence through multilateral MoU exchange.
  • About NABL: Established in 1998, headquartered in New Delhi, India.

1.16  NCLT Launched e-Inspection and e-Certified Copy Services To Enhance Access to Judicial Recordsp.21
Exam Hints:
  • What: Virtual Inauguration of two digital legal services of NCLT
  • Digital Services: e-Inspection and e-Certified Copy
  • Launched by: Justice Anupinder Singh Grewal, President of NCLT
  • Purpose: To provide access to judicial records digitally
Background:
  • These new digital legal services were inaugurated virtually by Justice Anupinder Singh Grewal, President of the NCLT.
About the Services:
  • Services: The system enables online inspection of case records and digital issuance of certified copies through the NCLT e-Filing portal, allowing registered users to submit requests and access approved documents digitally, ensuring faster and more convenient access for stakeholders.
  • Digitalisation: The services are part of the NCLT’s broader digital transformation programme, including a revamped website to strengthen digital infrastructure and enhance user experience.
  • Efficiency: A data-driven mechanism monitors pendency, enables workload redistribution and Special Benches, and incorporates improved case registration and listing guidelines, reducing delays, minimising physical visits, and ensuring a transparent, trackable system.
  • About NCLT: quasi-judicial body in India, established on June 1 2016 under Section 408 of the Companies Act, 2013 (18 of 2013).

1.17  NIELIT Launches Cyber Kushti 2026 National Cybersecurity AI Hackathon To Test Human Judgment Skillsp.21
Exam Hints:
  • What: Launch of Cyber Kushti 2026 – National Cybersecurity & AI Hackathon
  • By Whom: NIELIT, MeitY
  • Where: 3rd NCCDFI 2026, Delhi
  • Objective: Build cybersecurity skills in AI-driven environments
  • Organiser: NIELIT with ISAC Foundation, CERT-In
Background:
  • The initiative was launched as part of the technical programme of the 3rd National Conference on Cyber Security, Digital Forensics and Intelligence (NCCDFI 2026) held at Dr. Ambedkar International Centre in New Delhi, Delhi.
About Cyber Kushti 2026:
  • Objective: The hackathon aims to evaluate and recognise human judgment in cybersecurity by testing decision-making in AI-driven scenarios beyond simple vulnerability detection, with registration open until 10 September 2026.
  • Participation: The competition features three rounds: Akhada (15 September 2026), Dangal (24 September 2026), and the Kesari final (9 October 2026) and is open to students and independent researchers in teams of three, with free registration from 15 August to 10 September 2026.
  • Awards: Winning team receives “Cyber Kesari 2026” title and Gada, while runners-up receive “Rustam” and “Pahalwan”; participants get certificates and NSD credits.

1.18  NABARD Launches RSVC-AMRIT Platform for Rural Innovationp.22
Exam Hints:
  • What: Launch of RSVC-AMRIT
  • Launched by: Prof. Ajay Kumar Sood, Principal Scientific Adviser to GoI
  • Developed by: NABARD in collaboration with OPSA
  • Purpose: Rural innovation, technology transfer and adoption
Background:
  • The launch event was attended by Dr. Shaji Krishnan V., Chairman, NABARD, along with representatives from various stakeholder organisations.
Key Highlights of the RSVC-AMRIT Platform:
  • Digital Ecosystem: RSVC-AMRIT serves as the digital backbone of the RuTAGe Smart Village Centre (RSVC) ecosystem, enabling technology discovery, validation, deployment tracking, monitoring and knowledge management.
  • RSVC Initiative: The initiative transfers affordable technologies in sectors like agriculture, rural enterprises, renewable energy, education, water and sanitation, with NABARD supporting 8 RSVCs and developing Accelerated Medium for Rural Innovations and Technologies (AMRIT) as a national repository of rural innovations and best practices.
  • About NABARD: NABARD, established in 1982 and headquartered in Mumbai, Maharashtra, is India’s apex development financial institution for agriculture and rural development, headed by Chairman Dr. Shaji Krishnan V. (Shaji K.V.).

1.23  VP C. P. Radhakrishnan Launches India’s First Indigenous Non-GMO Popcorn Maize Hybrid in APp.26
Exam Hints:
  • What: Launch of India’s first indigenous non-GMO popcorn maize hybrid (Zea mays)
  • Launched By: C. P. Radhakrishnan, Vice-President of India
  • Location: Musunuru, Eluru district, AP
  • Developed By: ICAR-IIMR and Gourmet Popcornica
  • Significance: Reduces dependence on imported popcorn maize varieties
Background:
  • The event was attended by S. Abdul Nazeer, Governor of AP and N. Chandrababu Naidu, Chief Minister (CM) of AP.
About Indigenous Popcorn Maize Hybrid Seed:
  • Development: The hybrid seed was developed through a scientific and commercial collaboration between ndian Council of Agricultural Research-Indian Institute of Maize Research (ICAR–IIMR) and Gourmet Popcornica, initiated in February 2021.
  • Technology: The hybrid seed was developed using conventional non-GMO hybrid breeding methods, involving controlled cross-pollination of selected parent lines to combine desirable traits.
  • Significance: The indigenous hybrid seed will reduce India’s dependence on imported popcorn maize technologies, supports domestic popcorn production, value-added agricultural processing, and export opportunities for popcorn products.
  • About ICAR-IIMR: Established in 1957 and headquartered in Ludhiana, Punjab, it is focused on maize breeding, crop improvement, agronomy, value-chain development, and technology transfer.

1.24  IPC Launches 7 Nitrosamine Impurities Reference Standards for the First Timep.26
Exam Hints:
  • What: Launch of 7 Nitrosamine Impurities Reference Standards
  • Launched by: Indian Pharmacopoeia Commission (IPC)
  • Under: Ministry of Health & Family Welfare (MoHFW)
  • Event: IPC Interactive Meeting with CDL & CCL
  • Venue: Kolkata, West Bengal
  • Focus: “IP 2026: Perspectives, Suggestions & Way Forward”
  • Purpose: Accurate detection & quantification of nitrosamine impurities
  • 7 Reference Standards: NDMA, NDEA, NEIPA, NDBA, NMPA, NDIPA, and NMBA
Background:
  • These new reference standards were formally launched at an interactive meeting titled “IP 2026: Perspectives, Suggestions, & Way Forward”, organized by IPC at the Central Drugs Laboratory (CDL) and Central Cosmetics Laboratory (CCL)in Kolkata, West Bengal (WB).
Key Details of Reference Standards:
  • 07 Reference Standards: The new 7 reference standards are: N-Nitrosodimethylamine (NDMA), N-Nitrosodiethylamine (NDEA), N-Nitrosoethylisopropylamine (NEIPA), N-Nitrosodibutylamine (NDBA), N-Nitrosomethylphenylamine (NMPA), N-Nitrosodiisopropylamine (NDIPA), and N-Nitroso-N-methyl-4-aminobutyric acid (NMBA).
  • Carcinogenic Nitrosamine: Nitrosamines are highly potent genotoxic and carcinogenic compounds that may arise unintentionally during manufacturing, storage and packaging, or through contamination from materials used in pharmaceutical production.
About Indian Pharmacopeia Commission (IPC):
  • Established in 2005, IPC, an autonomous body under MoHFW, was fully operationalized in January, 2009.
  • Chairperson: Punya Salila Srivastava, Secretary, MoHFW

  • Policy Framework: Indian Space Policy 2023
  • Applicable To: Private space entities undertaking planned re-entry
  • Casualty Risk Limit: Less than 1 in 10,000
  • Insurance Requirement: Third-party liability insurance (if prescribed).
Background:
  • The guidelines were issued on 23 July 2026, made public on 29 July 2026, and released under the Indian Space Policy 2023 framework.
About New Re-entry Guidelines:
  • Authorisation: Indian entities require IN-SPACe approval for planned re-entry, while foreign entities must apply through an Indian incorporated entity.
  • Reporting: Companies must conduct re-entry at their own risk, obtain third-party liability insurance (if required), and declare planned re-entry during authorisation or seek approval six months before execution if decided after launch.
  • Significance: The guidelines strengthen space safety, debris management and accountability while supporting India’s private space sector growth.
  • About IN-SPACe: IN-SPACe, established in 2020 under the Department of Space (DoS), promotes and authorises private space activities.

1.39  Glaw Lake in Arunachal Pradesh Designated as India’s 101st Ramsar Sitep.40
Exam Hints:
  • What: Designation of Glaw Lake as India’s 101st Ramsar Site
  • Location: Tawang district, Arunachal Pradesh
  • India’s Ramsar Site Count: 101st
  • First Ramsar Site of: Arunachal Pradesh
  • Ecosystem Type: High-altitude Himalayan wetland
  • Governing Convention: Ramsar Convention on Wetlands, 1971
Ramsar Sites in the World:
  • Largest Site: Rio Negro in Brazil is the world’s largest Ramsar Site, covering approximately 120,000 sq km (square kilometers) (12 million hectares).
Ramsar Sites in India:
  • Global Rank: With the latest addition, India ranks first in Asia and third globally after the United Kingdom (UK) (176 sites) and Mexico (144 sites).
  • Top 3 Ramsar States: Tamil Nadu has the highest number of Ramsar Sites (20), followed by Uttar Pradesh (13). Punjab, Bihar, and Odisha jointly hold the third position with 6 Ramsar Sites each.
About Ramsar Convention on Wetlands:
  • Background: international treaty for wetland conservation and sustainable use, adopted in 1971 in Ramsar, Iran, and came into force in 1975.
  • Aim: It provides a framework for the conservation and sustainable use of wetlands across its 172 member countries, including India (which acceded to the treaty on February 01, 1982).
  • Total Ramsar Sites in the World: As of August 2026, the total number of Ramsar sites in the world is nearly 2,600 Secretary-General (SG)- Dr. Musonda Mumba
  • Headquarters: Gland, Switzerland.

1.40  ICAR Develops India’s First T2T Genome of Pigeonpea Variety ‘Asha’p.41
Exam Hints:
  • What: Development of India’s first fully annotated T2T reference genome of pigeonpea
  • Developed by: ICAR
  • Variety: ‘Asha’ (Cajanus cajan L. Millsp.)
  • Genome Assembly: Designated NIPB_CcT2T_4; deposited in NCBI database
  • Support: Mission for ‘Aatmanirbharta in Pulses’ (2025-26 to 2030-31) – target: 35 million tonnes pulse production by 2030-31
Background:
  • The breakthrough supports the Government of India’s (GoI) Mission for Aatmanirbharta in Pulses (2025–26 to 2030–31) to achieve a pulse production target of about 35 million tonnes (MT) by 2030–31.
About T2T Reference Genome of Pigeonpea:
  • Genome: The reference genome comprises 752.65 million DNA (Deoxyribonucleic Acid ) base pairs assembled into 92 contigs, representing all 11 chromosomes, including 11 centromeres and 22 telomeres, ensuring chromosome-level completeness.
  • Annotation: Scientists identified 36,557 genes producing 48,008 messenger RNA (mRNA) sequences, with transcriptome mapping recording over 99.9% read alignment, confirming the genome’s high accuracy.
  • Repository: The genome assembly, designated NIPB_CcT2T_4, was deposited in the National Center for Biotechnology Information (NCBI) database and released on 20 April 2026.

1.42  SCI Extends Mandatory Third-Party Insurance Cover for New Cars and Bikes by One Yearp.42
Exam Hints:
  • What: SCI extended mandatory third-party motor insurance for new vehicles
  • Judicial Bench: Justice Sanjay Karol & Justice Prashant Kumar Mishra
  • New Cars: 4-year third-party insurance
  • New Two-Wheelers: 6-year third-party insurance
  • Legal Provision: Section 146, Motor Vehicles Act (MVA), 1988
  • Implementation Agency: IRDAI.
Background:
  • New cars will now have 4 years of third-party insurance coverage, while two-wheelers will have 6 years of coverage.
About Mandatory Third-Party Insurance Rules:
  • Third-party motor insurance: mandatory insurance cover under the Motor Vehicles Act (MVA), 1988, provides legal liability cover for injury, death, or property damage caused to a third party by the insured vehicle.
  • Implementation: SCI directed the Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India (IRDAI) to implement the revised insurance periods immediately and proposed a four-layer insurance policy framework with mandatory third-party cover and optional add-ons.
  • Fuel Station Enforcement: Directed the IRDAI and the Ministry of Road Transport and Highways (MoRTH) to explore a pilot project for implementing a “No Insurance, No Fuel” policy at petrol pumps.
  • About IRDAI: Established in 1999 as a statutory body under the IRDA Act, 1999, and headquartered in Hyderabad, Telangana.

1.43  Squadron Leader Bhawana Kanth Becomes IAF’s First Woman Fighter Combat Leaderp.43
Exam Hints:
  • What: 1st woman fighter pilot of IAF
  • Who: Squadron Leader Bhawana Kanth
  • Where: TACDE, Gwalior, MP
  • Achievement: Successfully completed the Fighter Combat Leader (FCL) course
  • Previous Milestones: 1st woman fighter pilot qualified for day combat missions (2019);First woman fighter pilot to participate in the Republic Day parade (2021);
Background:
  • The achievement makes her the first woman graduate of the IAF’s elite “Top Gun” school, where only about 1% of IAF pilots are selected for the 20-week advanced combat programme.
About Squadron Leader Bhawana Kanth:
  • Background: An engineering graduate from Darbhanga, Bihar, she was commissioned into the IAF in June 2016 as one of India’s first three women fighter pilots alongside Mohana Singh and Avani Chaturvedi.
  • Career: She initially flew the MiG-21 Bison before transitioning to the Sukhoi Su-30 MKI, becoming the first woman fighter pilot qualified for daytime combat missions in 2019, the first to participate in the Republic Day parade in 2021 and flew in the Republic Day flypast in 2024.
  • Qualification: FCL qualification is awarded after completing the TACDE’s 20-week advanced combat course, enabling pilots to lead complex air combat operations and tactical missions.
  • About Indian Air Force (IAF): IAF, established on 8 October 1932 and headquartered in New Delhi (Delhi), is the air arm of the Indian Armed Forces.

About PM E-DRIVE Scheme:

  • Allocation: PM E-DRIVE Scheme has a total outlay of Rs 11,900 crore, including Rs 2,767 crore in fund support for up to 45,79,120 registered electric two-wheelers and Rs 55 crore for administrative expenses.
  • Incentive: For E-2Ws priced up to Rs 1.5 lakh ex-factory, the incentive is Rs 5,000/kWh capped at Rs 10,000 per vehicle in the Financial Year 2024–25 (FY25), and Rs 2,500/kWh capped at Rs 5,000 per vehicle from April 1, 2025 to March 31, 2028.
  • Timeline: The terminal date for all scheme segments is March 31, 2028, while claims for benefits must be submitted to the Ministry or Project Management Agency by December 31, 2027.

1.48  President Murmu Gives Assent To Bill Criminalising Insult To Vande Mataramp.48
Exam Hints:
  • What: President gives assent to criminalise insult to Vande Mataram
  • Act Amended: Section 3 of Prevention of Insults to National Honours Act, 1971
  • Prohibited: Prevent singing of Vande Mataram, Disturbance to assembly singing
  • Punishment: 3 years imprisonment
  • Repeated Convictions: Enhanced penalty of minimum 1 year
Key Amendments:
  • Act amended: Prevention of Insults to National Honour (Amendment) Bill, 2026 amends Section 3 of the Prevention of Insults to National Honour Act, 1971.
  • Protection provided: It extends the same legal protection to the National Song, Vande Mataram that is currently afforded to the National Anthem, Jana Gana Mana.
  • Punishment: Imprisonment for a term which may extend to three years, or with fine, or with both.
Background Information:
  • Timeline: Prevention of Insults to National Honour (Amendment) Bill, 2026 was introduced in Rajya Sabha on July 24, 2026.Then the bill was passed on July 29,2026 in Rajya Sabha and July 30,2026 in Lok Sabha.
About Vande Mataram:
  • Vande Mataram is India’s national song, composed in Sanskrit by Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay in November 1875.It was first sung by Rabindranath Tagore at the 1896 Congress Session in Calcutta.It was adopted as India’s National Song by the Constituent Assembly in 1950.

  • Recognition: The achievement reflects MIDHANI’s technical excellence, commitment to global quality standards, and advanced materials testing capabilities.
  • About MIDHANI: Established in 1973, MIDHANI is a Miniratna Category-I Schedule-B Public Sector Undertaking (PSU) and is headquartered in Hyderabad, Telangana.

1.57  GoI Approves Incentive Scheme for Promotion of Domestic PNG Connectionsp.60
Exam Hints:
  • What: Approval of incentive scheme for domestic PNG connections
  • Approved By: Government of India
  • Effective From: 1 September 2026
  • Beneficiaries: City Gas Distribution (CGD) companies
  • Nodal Ministry: Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas (MoPNG)
  • Key Incentive: Additional 200 Standard Cubic Metre (SCM) of APM gas per incremental billed domestic PNG connection
  • Implementation: Two tranches over six months
Background:
  • India currently has around 1.74 crore domestic PNG connections.
Scheme Framework:
  • Incentive Mechanism: Eligible CGD entities will receive an additional allocation of 200 Standard Cubic Metres (SCM) of lower-priced domestically produced Administered Pricing Mechanism (APM) / Non-Administered Price Mechanism (NAPM) gas for every incremental billed domestic PNG connection.
  • Impact: The scheme is expected to reduce the payback period for capital expenditure on domestic PNG connections from around 10 years to approximately 3 years.
GoI Initiatives:
  • LPG-PNG Transition: In May 2026, the GoI amended the Liquefied Petroleum Gas (Regulation of Supply and Distribution) Order, 2026, allowing LPG consumers shifting to PNG either to terminate their LPG connection within 30 days or obtain a transfer voucher for restoring it later in a non-PNG area.
Benefits of Domestic PNG:
  • Safety & Environment-Friendly: PNG is supplied via low-pressure underground pipelines, minimizing risks from Liquefied Petroleum Gas (LPG) cylinders.

1.61  President Droupadi Murmu Assent to Taxation & Payment and Settlement Systems (Amendment) Laws, 2026p.64
Exam Hints:
  • What: President assents to Taxation and Other Laws (Amendment) Act, 2026 and PSS Act 2007
  • IT Exemption: On interest earned and Capital gains from Government securities,
  • Diamond business: Income from sale of rough diamonds exempted
  • Electronic Components
  • Exemption: Income from storage of components in warehouse
  • Eligibility: Mobile phones, laptops, servers, sub-assemblies of finished goods
  • Validity: For 15 years, till 31st March 2041
  • SPV Trust: Surcharge on IT – 25%
  • PSS Act: Committee headed by NPCI to decide MDR charges
Taxation and Other Laws Act, 2026 Amendment:
  • Old Taxation: Previously, Under the Income-tax Act, interest income was taxed at 20%, short-term capital gains at 30%, and long-term capital gains at 12.5%.
Tax Exemption for Electronic Components:
  • Exemption: Income earned by foreign companies from the storage of components in warehouses located in a customs-bonded area will be exempt from tax.
  • Validity: The exemption will apply to income arising on or after October 1, 2026, and will remain available for a period of 15 years that is until March 31, 2041.
  • Another Exemption: Income-tax (IT) Act exempts income of a foreign company arising from providing capital goods, equipment, or tooling until 2040-41.
Investment related amendment:
  • Investment funds registered outside India: Several conditions for tax exemption of eligible investment funds registered outside India but managed from India are removed:Minimum 25 membersMaximum 10% participation by a single investorMinimum monthly average corpus of Rs 100 croreMaximum 25% investment in a single entity

1.64  CMLRE Releases National Report on Deep-Sea and Distant-Water Fishery Resources in Indian EEZp.67
Exam Hints:
  • What: National Report on Deep-Sea and Distant-Water Fishery Resources of the Indian EEZ Released
  • Who: CMLRE, MoES
  • When: During Silver Jubilee celebrations of CMLRE
  • Edited by: Dr V. N. Sanjeevan and Dr Baban Ingole, published by Dr R. S. Maheshkumar
  • Objective: Roadmap for sustainable development of Blue economy
  • Programme: Marine Living Resources Programme
  • Vessel Used: FORV Sagar Sampada
  • Hotspots Identified: Kollam Bank; Angria Bank; Mangalore slope; Trivandrum terrace; Lakshadweep; A&N Islands.
Highlights of the Event:
  • Research Legacy: The report brings together nearly four decades of pioneering oceanographic research and three decades of systematic surveys across India’s 2.37 million (Mn) square kilometre (Sq Km) Exclusive Economic Zone (EEZ).
  • Based on: The report’s findings are based on an extensive research effort, comprising more than 400 oceanographic cruises undertaken since the 1990s under the Marine Living Resources Programme (MLRP).
Research Highlights:
  • Twilight Zone: The report highlights the 200-1000 metre(m) mesopelagic zone, which supports abundant lanternfishes (myctophids), deep-sea shrimps and cephalopods.They play a key role in the biological carbon pump, transporting carbon from surface waters to the deep sea.
  • Deep-sea Resources: It identifies underexploited deep-sea resources, including shrimps, perches, scombroids, flatfishes and eels beyond 200 m.
About Centre for Marine Living Resources & Ecology (CMLRE):
  • Director: Dr R.S. Maheshkumar
  • Headquarters: Kochi, Kerala
  • Established: 1998

1.65  DGFT Eased Rupee invoicing rules for exporters under FTPp.68
Exam Hints:
  • What: Relaxation in Rupee-based export transactions
  • Relaxed by: DGFT, MoCI
  • Amendment: Certain Provisions of FTP 2023
  • Aligns With: RBI’s Foreign Exchange Management (Manner of Receipt and Payment) Regulations, 2023
  • Key Benefit: Exporters can invoice and receive payments in INR or foreign currencies
  • ACU Rule: Separate mechanism for ACU members
  • Exception: Nepal & Bhutan.
Background:
  • The relaxation was provided through amendment of certain provisions of the Foreign Trade Policy (FTP 2023), aligning with the Reserve Bank of India (RBI)’s Foreign Exchange Management (Manner of Receipt and Payment) Regulations, 2023.
Key Changes:
  • Flexibility in Export Contracts: As per the revised FTP, export contracts and invoices for countries outside the Asian Clearing Union (ACU), may now be denominated in any foreign currency or INR.
  • Special Inclusion: Further, exports financed through Export Import Bank of India (EXIM) Bank or the Government of India (GoI) lines of credit may be invoiced in INR.
About Asian Clearing Union (ACU):
  • Formation: regional payment mechanism created in 1974 to facilitate trade settlements between its members.
  • Members: It comprises 9 member countries, including Bangladesh, Bhutan, India, Iran, Maldives, Myanmar, Nepal, Pakistan and Sri Lanka; and each member country is represented by its central bank.
  • Secretary General: Mojtaba Khalesi
  • Headquarters: Tehran, Iran

1.68  NBSS&LUP Develops India’s First Soil Texture Map for Precision Agriculturep.71
Exam Hints:
  • What: India’s first hectare-level Soil Texture Map Released
  • Developed by: NBSS&LUP
  • Technology: Remote Sensing, Satellite Data & AI
  • Maps: Sand, silt & clay proportions.
Background:
  • The map, released in July 2026, provides hectare-level soil information to support crop planning, irrigation management, and land-use decisions.
About India’s 1st Soil Texture Map:
  • Soil Texture: It refers to the proportion of sand, silt and clay in soil, which determines its water-holding capacity, drainage, aeration and suitability for different crops.
  • Technology: The map was prepared using field studies, remote sensing, satellite imagery, and Artificial Intelligence (AI) by integrating soil and geospatial data.
  • Applications: The map supports crop selection, irrigation planning, soil classification, and precision agriculture by helping farmers make location-specific decisions.

1.73  Mizoram Declares 79 Monuments and Archaeological Sites as State Protected Monumentsp.75
Exam Hints:
  • What: Declared 79 Monuments and Archaeological sites as State Protected Monuments
  • Who: Mizoram
  • Under: Mizoram Ancient Monuments and Archaeological sites and remains act, 2001
  • ASI Sites: Kawtchhuah Ropui, Ralven Puk; Lianpui Hmun (Champhai)
  • Proposed Sites: Reng Puk, Lung Milem, Pipute Lungdawh, Ruhro Puk.
About State Protected Monuments:
  • Sites Details: Kawtchhuah Ropui and Ralven Puk were declared Monuments of National Importance by the ASI in 2012 and Lianpui Hmun received the ASI tag in 2025.Kawtchhuah Ropui (Great Entranceway) is an important archaeological site with over 170 intricately carved standing stones (menhirs)Ralven Puk is a rock-cut cave traditionally used by Mizo ancestors as a lookout/watchpost during conflicts.
  • Protection Proposals: Four additional heritage sites proposed for designation as protected monuments:Reng Puk and Lung Milem in Mamit district,Pipute Lungdawh in Champhai district, andRuhro Puk in Saitual district.
  • Note: Mizoram Ancient Monuments and Archaeological Sites and Remains Act, 2001 is a State law of Mizoram enacted to provide for the preservation, protection and conservation of ancient monuments and archaeological sites and remains of historical, archaeological or artistic interest in Mizoram.
About Mizoram:
  • Chief Minister (CM)– Pu Lalduhoma
  • Capital: Aizawl National Parks (NPs)– Murlen NP, Phawngpui NP

  • By: Hindalco Industries Limited
  • Where: Belagavi, Karnataka
  • Capacity: 30,000 tonnes per annum
  • Feature: First in India; fully powered by renewable energy
  • Use: Halogen-free flame-retardant material for cables, EVs & insulation.
Background:
  • The greenfield facility marks the first domestic production of this high-value specialty alumina product, strengthening India’s capability in fire-safety materials and supporting the Atmanirbhar Bharat initiative.
About India’s First PPT ATH Plant:
  • Overview: The plant in Belagavi, Karnataka, has a capacity of 30,000 tonnes per annum, expandable to 60,000 tonnes, and runs entirely on renewable energy.
  • Product: PPT ATH is a Halogen-Free Flame-Retardant (HFFR) material used in cables, insulation, and polymers to suppress flames, reduce smoke, and prevent toxic gas emission.
  • Technology: The facility produces Hindalco’s InnoSafe Superfine PPT ATH, developed through in-house Research and Development (R&D), featuring automated process controls and an integrated system to ensure consistent quality and feedstock efficiency.
  • About Hindalco Industries: Established in 1958 and headquartered in Mumbai, Maharashtra, is led by Managing Director (MD) Satish Pai.

  • Records: Council will maintain an electronic depository of mediated settlement agreements and related records, while preparing and submitting reports on the implementation of the Mediation Act, 2023 to the Central Government.
  • About Mediation Act 2023: In 2023, the Parliament enacted the Mediation Act, 2023, which requires individuals to try and resolve civil or commercial disputes through mediation before any court or tribunal.Act mandates the mediation process to be completed within 180 days (or, 6 months), with the possibility of extending it to another 180 days, if both parties agree.

  • Slogan: The slogan of PMJDY is “Mera Khata – Bhagya Vidhata” (My Account – My Fortune).

3.3  Karnataka Cabinet Approves Sandhya Kiran Scheme Offering Rs 5 Lakh Cashless Healthcare Cover to Pensionersp.90
Exam Hints:
  • What: Approval of Sandhya Kiran cashless healthcare scheme
  • By whom: Karnataka Cabinet
  • Under: Ayushman Bharat–Arogya Karnataka
  • Objective: Provide healthcare security to pensioners
  • Coverage: Rs 5 lakh per family/year; 3.11 lakh pensioners; Rs. 117 crore annual contributions against an estimated Rs. 81.75 crore cost
  • Focus: Cashless treatment at empanelled hospitals
Background:
  • The scheme will cover around 4.93 lakh beneficiaries, including 3.11 lakh state pensioners and their dependents, with Rs.5 lakh annual family health cover and standardised treatment packages and rates.
About Sandhya Kiran Scheme:
  • Benefits: The scheme provides coverage for secondary, tertiary, and emergency treatments at empanelled hospitals and aims to ensure financial and health security while reducing out-of-pocket medical expenses.
  • Funding: The contributory model includes 1.25% from service pensioners and 0.75% from family pensioners, generating Rs.117 crore annually against an estimated Rs.81.75 crore cost with 70:30 beneficiary–government sharing.
  • Management: Contributions will increase by 0.05% points if utilisation exceeds 85% for sustainability, while Suvarna Arogya Suraksha Trust handles enrolment, empanelment, and claims processing.

  • The launch event was attended by Union Minister of State (MoS) Anupriya Patel, Ministry of Health and Family Welfare (MoHFW).
About Ayushman Bharat PM-JAY:
  • Overview: centrally sponsored health assurance scheme launched by the Government of India (GoI) in September 2018 under the Ayushman Bharat (AB) initiative.It is implemented by the National Health Authority (NHA) under the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare (MoHFW).The scheme provides health coverage of up to Rs. 5 lakh per family per year for secondary and tertiary care hospitalisation to eligible families.
  • Coverage: The scheme will be available across around 2,000 government and private hospitals in West Bengal, covering all citizens aged above 70, including around 35 lakh elderly beneficiaries in the state.
  • State Health Scheme: Mukhyamantri Swasthya Bima Yojana was launched to provide health insurance coverage to families not covered under Ayushman Bharat, targeting around 80 lakh families in WB.

4.1  Saudi Arabia & 13 Other Countries Announced Maritime Defence Alliance to Protect Ships in West Asia Waterwaysp.94
Exam Hints:
  • What: Formation of Maritime Defence Alliance
  • Key Purpose: To protect shipping in key West Asian waterways
  • Key Waterways: Red Sea, the Bab el-Mandeb Strait and the Gulf of Aden
  • Total Founding Members: 14, including Bangladesh, Pakistan, Egypt, among others
  • Alliance Leader: Saudi Arabia
  • Permanent Headquarters: Saudi Arabia
Background:
  • The announcement followed an international maritime security meeting hosted by Saudi Arabia in Riyadh, attended by chiefs of staff and representatives from 43 countries and the European Union (EU).
About Maritime Defence Alliance:
  • Key Objectives: The new alliance will serve as a framework for maritime defence cooperation, aimed to enhance maritime security, protect freedom of navigation, secure international maritime routes and energy supply lines in the Red Sea, the Bab el-Mandeb Strait and the Gulf of Aden.
  • Nature of Alliance: The newly announced maritime alliance is purely defensive in nature which means it will not target any state, respecting the state sovereignty, and will conduct its activities in compliance with international law, ensuring freedom of navigation.
  • Founding Member and Secretariat: Saudi Arabia designated as the founding and leading state of the alliance and serves as its permanent secretariat.
About Saudi Arabia:
  • Prime Minister (PM)- Crown Prince Mohammed bin Salman Al Saud
  • Capital: Riyadh

4.2  Venezuela Withdraws from the Rome Statute, Ending ICC Membershipp.94
Exam Hints:
  • What: Withdrawal from the Rome Statute
  • Who: Venezuela
  • International Body: International Criminal Court (ICC)
  • Submission: Notification submitted to UN
  • Reason: Alleged ICC geographical bias and political instrumentalization.
  • Legal Basis: Under Article 127, withdrawal takes effect one year after notification, does not affect ongoing investigations.
About Venezuela’s Withdrawal from the Rome Statute:
  • Investigation: Since 2021, the ICC has investigated alleged crimes against humanity in Venezuela arising from the 2017 anti-government protest crackdown, including alleged torture, arbitrary detention, sexual violence and other inhumane acts by state security forces and pro-government groups against civilians.
  • About Chad’s Withdrawal: On 27 July 2026, Chad notified the UN Secretary-General of its decision to withdraw from the Rome Statute under Article 127, citing the ICC’s alleged selective focus on African countries.
About Rome Statute:
  • Foundation: Rome Statute is the international treaty that established the ICC, comprising a Preamble and 13 Parts; it was adopted on 17 July 1998 and entered into force on 1 July 2002.
  • Jurisdiction: It empowers the ICC to prosecute genocide, crimes against humanity, war crimes, and, following the 2010 amendment, the crime of aggression.
About International Criminal Court (ICC):
  • President: Judge Tomoko Akane (Japan)
  • Headquarters: Hague, Netherlands
  • Established: 2002 under the Rome Statute

4.4  India Donates Five Peacocks to UNOG Including Rare White-Feathered Malep.96
Exam Hints:
  • What: India donated 5 peacocks
  • To: UNOG, Geneva, Switzerland
  • Total birds: 5 (4 blue peafowl + 1 white male)
  • Purpose: Promote biodiversity conservation.
Background:
  • The initiative highlights India’s natural heritage and commitment to global environmental stewardship, with the group comprising four blue peafowl and one white-feathered male symbolizing the UNs blue and white colours.
About India’s Peacock Donation to UNOG:
  • Ceremony: The birds were formally handed over by Arindam Bagchi, India’s Permanent Representative to the UN in Geneva, to Tatiana Valovaya, Director-General (DG) of the UNOG.
  • Habitat: The peacocks will remain in an enclosed aviary for about three months before being released into the 46-hectare Ariana Park within the UN premises in Geneva.
  • Background: This marks India’s second such contribution after the 1981 donation by former Prime Minister (PM) Indira Gandhi, who sent a breeding pair from Delhi Zoo.
  • Tradition: Peacocks have inhabited Ariana Park for over a century, a tradition dating back to 1890 when Gustave Revilliod bequeathed the estate to the City of Geneva, with care now overseen by the Geneva Bioparc zoo and UN staff.

BANKING & FINANCE • RBI IN NEWS
6.1  RBI Delays Implementation of Basel III Disclosure Rules to April 1, 2027p.107
Exam Hints:
  • What: RBI delays the implementation of Basel III disclosure
  • Old Deadline: September 2026
  • New Deadline: April 1, 2027
  • Alignment: Align disclosure requirements with ECL
  • Important amendments: Board approved formal disclosure policy
  • Disclosure apply at top consolidate level
  • Templates for market, operational and counterparty credit risk
  • Quarterly disclosure – Period ending June 30,2027
  • Half-yearly disclosure – Period ending September 30,2027
  • Annual Disclosure – March 31, 2028
  • State owned banks exempted from disclosing Chief Executives remuneration
  • Archive Period for Pillar 3 reports – 5 years
Key Details:
  • Applicable to: RBI issued 10 amendment directions revising its Basel Pillar 3 disclosure framework for commercial banks (CB), small finance banks (SFB) and payments banks (PB).
  • Old timeline: RBI had initially proposed implementing the final directions from the quarter ended September 30, 2026.
Important Amendments:
  • Quarterly and annual disclosures: Following the new deadline, the first round of quarterly disclosures will be submitted for the period ending June 30, 2027.Half-yearly disclosures will commence with the period ending September 30, 2027, and annual disclosures are slated to begin for the fiscal year concluding March 31, 2028.
  • Archive Period: RBI has reduced the mandatory archive period for Pillar 3 reports on banks’ websites from 10 years to at least 5 years for prior reporting periods.
About BASEL III Framework:
  • Introduced in: Basel Committee on Banking Supervision (BCBS) introduced the Basel III framework in 2010 to address deficiencies exposed during the 2008 financial crisis.

External Sector:

NBFC-ICC: Non-Banking Financial Company – Investment and Credit Company; HFC: Housing Finance Company;
NBFC – Scale Based Regulation (SBR):
  • Base Layer: Base Layer shall comprise of:Non-deposit taking NBFCs below the asset size of Rs 1,000 Cr,NBFCs undertaking the following activities:NBFC-Peer to Peer Lending Platform (NBFC-P2P),NBFC-Account Aggregator (NBFC-AA),Non-Operative Financial Holding Company (NOFHC)
  • Upper Layer: Upper Layer shall consist of NBFCs having asset size of Rs 1,00,000 Cr and above per the latest audited balance sheet for the financial year.The asset size threshold for identification of NBFC-UL shall be reviewed every three years.An NBFC-UL shall be mandatorily listed within three years of its identification by RBI in the Upper Layer.
  • Top Layer: Top Layer will ideally remain empty.

6.6  RBI Imposes Rs. 59.20 Lakh Monetary Penalty on IndusInd Bank for Regulatory Non- Compliancep.115
Exam Hints:
  • What: RBI imposed a monetary penalty on IndusInd Bank
  • Penalty Amount: Rs.59.20 lakh
  • Act: Banking Regulation Act, 1949
  • Regulation: Section 47A(1)(c) read with section 46(4)(i) of the Banking Regulation Act, 1949
  • Reason: Non-compliance with RBI directions on deposit rates and securitisation of standard assets.
Background:
  • This penalty imposed in exercise of powers conferred on RBI under the provisions of Section 47A(1)(c) read with section 46(4)(i) of the Banking Regulation Act, 1949.
About RBI Action:
  • Reasons for Penalty: RBI identified two major regulatory violations by IndusInd Bank: payment of interest on certain current account deposits in violation of RBI directions and undertaking activities in the nature of Synthetic Securitisation of standard assets against RBI guidelines.
  • Compliance: The penalty was imposed following the Statutory Inspection for Supervisory Evaluation (ISE) conducted by the RBI with reference to IndusInd Bank’s financial position as of 31 March 2025.
  • About IndusInd Bank Limited: Established in 1994, it is headquartered in Mumbai, Maharashtra, and carries the tagline “We Make You Feel Richer”.

6.7  RBI Relaxes Weekly Access Rule for FCNR(B) Swap Facility for Transactions Above USD 100 Millionp.116
Exam Hints:
  • What: FCNR(B) swap rule relaxed
  • By Whom: RBI
  • Objective: To manage liquidity, speed transfers, and stabilise forex and the rupee
  • Threshold: Above USD 100 million for flexible access
  • Total inflows: USD 72.85 billion (as of August 21, 2026).
About FCNR(B) Swap Facility Relaxation:
  • Features: Banks can now access the swap facility on any day for inflows exceeding USD 100 million, while smaller transactions remain on weekly schedules, improving liquidity management, reducing hedging costs, easing short-term swap pressures, and enabling efficient handling of surplus dollar liquidity.
  • Data: The facility attracted USD 72.85 billion in inflows, with FCNR(B) deposits contributing USD 65.40 billion, followed by overseas borrowings of USD 4.86 billion and External Commercial Borrowings (ECB) of USD 2.59 billion .
  • Timeline: The scheme began on June 8, 2026, with deposit mobilisation permitted until August 31, 2026 and swap access until September 11, 2026, while ECB and Offshore Foreign Currency Borrowings (OFCB) windows remain open until December 31, 2026.
  • Tenure: The deposits are typically maintained for a minimum period of 1 year and up to 5 years.

  • Purpose: Make digital platforms accessible to persons with disabilities.

  • Remediation of those audit findings – July, 31
  • New Timeline: October 31, 2026 News_2:
  • What: SEBI launches GARUDA Mechanism
  • Purpose: Fasten the AIF launch Process
  • Launch Timeline: Existing AIFs: 10 working days from filing PPM
  • New AIF: After SEBI registration/10 working days from filing PPM
  • Relaxation: AI-only funds, LVFs, Angel Funds
SEBI Accessibility Framework:
  • Previous Timeline: Earlier, the deadline to complete the digital platform accessibility audit was April 30 and the timeline to complete the remediation of those audit findings and achieve full compliance was July, 31.
GARUDA Mechanism in Detail:
  • In July 2026, SEBI issued a circular for Green-Channel: AIF Rollout Upon Document Acknowledgement (GARUDA) to streamline and expedite the launch process of AIF schemes.
  • Existing AIFs: Existing AIFs may launch a new scheme within 10 working days of filing the Private Placement Memorandum (PPM) with SEBI through a registered merchant banker.
  • New AIFs: Newly registered AIFs may launch their first scheme only after obtaining SEBI registration or 10 working days from the date of filing the PPM.

6.9  SEBI Proposes Mandatory Colour-Coded Credit Risk-o-Meter for Debt Securitiesp.118
Exam Hints:
  • What: SEBI proposes a colour-coded Credit Risk-o-Meter for debt securities
  • Purpose: Simplify assessment and comparison of credit risk
  • Applicable to: NCS, CPs, SDIs, SRs, MLDs and OBPPs
  • Disclosure: Offer documents, abridged prospectuses, private placement memorandums, advertisements, and OBPP web/mobile platforms
  • Data Source: Credit Rating Agencies
  • Levels: Six, AAA to D
  • Six Levels: Lowest credit risk, very low credit risk, low credit risk, moderate credit risk, moderate risk of default, and high to very high risk of default.
Background:
  • The proposal builds on SEBI’s existing Risk-o-Meter framework for Mutual Funds (MFs), which classifies risk into six levels from Low to Very High.
About Proposed Credit Risk-o-Meter:
  • Risk Clarification: The regulator stressed that the meter would indicate only credit risk and would not amount to investment advice.
  • Warning: SEBI has proposed a separate warning for unsecured perpetual bonds such as Additional Tier 1 (AT1) bonds, which may carry the risk of total loss of invested capital.
Proposed Levels:
  • Colour Coded: The proposal seeks to translate existing alphanumeric credit ratings such as AAA, AA+ and BBB- into a standardised, colour-coded visual scale.
  • Six levels: Under the proposed framework, the Credit Risk-o-Meter will map credit ratings into six risk levels: 1. Lowest credit risk 2. Very low credit risk 3. Low credit risk 4.
About Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI):
  • SEBI, a market regulator, was constituted as a non-statutory body on April 12,1988 through a resolution of the Government of India (GoI) and was established as a statutory body in 1992.
  • Chairman: Tuhin Kanta Pandey

6.11  SEBI Introduces IT Resilience Index Framework for Market Infrastructure Institutionsp.121
Exam Hints:
  • What: Introduces ITRI framework for MII
  • Who: SEBI
  • Objective: To measure health of MII IT systems
  • Coverage: Stock exchanges, depositories, clearing corporations
  • Parameters: 9
  • Maximum weightage: Availability and Security – 20%
  • Minimum weightage: Scalability – 5%
  • Implementation: March 31, 2027
  • Computation: Half Yearly.
ITRI Framework Overview:
  • Weightage: The availability and security parameter has the maximum weightage of 20% each.Integrity, governance, reliability and monitoring, business continuity, and modularity and flexibility are each assigned a weightage of 10%.On the other hand, the Scalability parameter has a weightage of 5%.
  • Finalization: Industry Standards Forum (ISF) must finalize sub-parameters and detailed measurement criteria by November 30, 2026, along with baseline parameters, threshold scores and an Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) for system-driven scoring.
  • Implementation: EWS and Real Time Monitoring of Service Delivery, must be operationalized by February 28, 2027, with the first ITRI computation covering the half-year ending March 31, 2027.

6.23  Niyo Forex and GlobalPay Secured Perpetual AD II Licence from RBIp.130
Exam Hints:
  • What: Granting of Perpetual AD-II licence
  • To: Niyo Forex via its Kanji Forex Pvt. Ltd.
  • Granted by: RBI
  • Significance: Long-term regulatory continuity for forex business;No need of fresh approvals from RBI for expansion of branch network;Expanded AD-II licence to: WSFx Global Pay (Global Pay)Maximum Remittance Limit: Rs 25 lakh (including both inward and outward remittances)
About Authorised Dealer Category (AD-II) licence:
  • Categories: RBI has classified ADs into three different categories:ADs of Category-I (AD-I): These are select banks who are allowed to undertake all permissible current and capital account transactions as per RBI directions issued from time to time.ADs of Category-II (AD-II): These are select entities to conduct specific non-trade related current account transactions and all the activities permitted to Full Fledged Money Changers (FFMCs) as per RBI directions.ADs of Category-III(AD-III): It involves select financial and other institutions to conduct specific foreign exchange transactions incidental to their business or activities.
  • Eligibility Criteria: To obtain AD-II licences, the interested financial entity is required to have minimum net worth of Rs 10 crore; and must have functioned as an FFMC for forex correspondent for minimum 2 years (for non-bank entities).
About Niyo Forex:
  • Headquarters: Mumbai, Maharashtra
  • Established in: 1998

6.32  UPI Completes 10 Years of Digital Payment Revolutionp.135
Exam Hints:
  • What: UPI completes 10 years of digital payment revolution
  • Launched on: 25 August 2016 by NPCI
  • UPI share: 84% in India in FY26
  • Global Share: 49% in 2025
  • Decade growth: 1.78 Cr transaction in FY17 to 24,162 Cr transactions in FY26
  • Value Growth: Rs 0.07 lakh Cr in FY17 to Rs 314 lakh Cr in FY26
  • Bank Expansion: 44 in FY17 to 703 in FY26.
About Unified Payments Interface (UPI):
  • Launch: UPI service was launched on 25 August 2016 by the NPCI under the regulatory oversight of the Reserve Bank of India (RBI).
  • Share of UPI: The share of UPI in India’s digital Payments is 84% in Financial Year 2025-26 (FY26).
UPI Growth & Expansion Trends:
  • Growth: Annual transaction volume expanded from just 1.78 crore (Cr) transactions in Financial Year 2016-17 (FY17) to over 24,162 Cr transactions in FY26, representing an almost 13,000-fold surge in transaction volume.
  • Transaction Value: At the same time, transaction value rose sharply from Rs 0.07 lakh Cr in FY17 to approximately Rs 314 lakh Cr in FY26, translating into a more than 4,000-fold increase in transaction value.
Transaction Analysis:
  • Below Rs 500: 86% of P2M transactions were below Rs 500, reflecting UPI’s widespread use for small-ticket, routine retail and day-to-day payments.59% of P2P transactions were below Rs 500, while 41% exceeded Rs 500, highlighting UPI’s use for both low-value personal payments and higher-value fund transfers.
About National Payments Corporation of India (NPCI):
  • Established: 2008

Key Details of Partnership:

7.1  SBI Research report: India’s economy is likely to grow around 7% in Q1 FY27p.143
Exam Hints:
  • What: Release of Pre-MPC Report
  • Released by: SBI Research
  • India’s GDP Projection: Around 7% (Q1FY27)
  • Growth Rate of Economic Indicators: domestic passenger vehicle sales (+24.1% Y–o-Y); electricity demand (+11.5%); exports (+15.5%) and IIP (+7.3%)
  • Inflation in Q1FY27: 3.9%
Key Findings:
  • Pick-up in High Frequency Indicators: The report highlighted growth in high-frequency indicators such as: domestic passenger vehicle sales (+24.1% Year-on-Year (Y–o-Y) in June 2026); electricity demand (+11.5%); exports (+15.5%) and the Index of Industrial Production (IIP) (+7.3%).
  • Inflation: Despite positive growth, India’s inflation for Q1FY27 stood at 3.9%.The report projected that Consumer Price Inflation (CPI) will remain above 5% during the next two quarters of FY27.However, inflation is projected to be at 5% for full FY27, which remains within the RBI’s target range of 2%-6%.
  • Monsoon Relief: The report noted that recovery in monsoon conditions i.e. surplus rainfall in the month of July 2026 reduced the nationwide rainfall deficit to nearly 13%, while reservoir levels returned to normal, among others, are expected to support economic activity in the coming months.
  • About SBI Research: SBI Research is the research and economic analysis wing of the State Bank of India (SBI).

7.6  SBI Research Report: Projects India’s GDP growth at 8% for Q1FY27p.146
Exam Hints:
  • What: Release of Ecowrap report
  • Released by: SBI Research
  • India’s GDP: At around 8% (Q1FY27)
  • Key Drivers: resilient consumption, strong industrial growth and positive growth in service sectors
Key Details:
  • Key Drivers: One of the key drivers for India’s higher growth estimate is strong consumer demand across several high-frequency indicators such as: domestic passenger vehicle sales (+24.1% Year-on-Year, Y-o-Y in June 2026); consumer credit (+15.8% in June 2026); electricity demand (11.5%); among others.Industry Growth: In addition, the Index of Industrial Production (IIP) witnessed a growth of 7.3% (in June 2026); up from 5% (in May 2026) and 4.9% (in April 2026).Service Sector: Services sector contributed significantly for India’s stronger Q1 GDP outlook as service PMI stood at 57.3 (in June 2026); while service exports increased to 13.3%; airport cargo traffic rose 22.3% and port cargo traffic increased 9.3% during the same time period.Capex Support: The report noted that capital expenditure by 20 states increased 5.5% Y-o-Y in Q1FY27, while the central government capex increased 23.7%, accounting for 27.8% of Budget Estimates (BE) in Q1FY27.

Exam Hints:
  • What: ICRA projects India’s GDP growth
  • GDP Growth Estimate (Q1 FY27): 7% from 7.8% (Q4 FY26)
  • Full FY27 GDP Growth Forecast: 6.7% from 7.7% (FY26)
  • Sector (Q1 FY27): Industrial Sector – 7.7%; Agriculture – 4%; Service Sector – 7.9%
Key Highlights of Sector-wise Economic Outlook:
  • Sector-wise Growth Estimate: ICRA projected industrial sector growth at 7.7%, agriculture, forestry and fishing growth at 4%, and services sector growth at 7.9% in Q1 FY27.
  • GVA: ICRA expects services Gross Value Added (GVA) growth to moderate to 7.9% in Q1 FY27 from 9.9% in the previous quarter.
  • FY27 Growth Forecast: ICRA retained India’s FY27 GDP growth forecast at 6.7%, lower than 7.7% in FY26, based on an average crude oil price assumption of USD 80–85 per barrel.

7.9  Ind–Ra Raised India’s FY27 Economic Growth Forecast By 10 Bps To 6.8%p.147
Exam Hints:
  • What: India’s GDP projection for FY27
  • By Whom: Ind-Ra
  • India’s GDP: 6.8% (+10 bps)
  • Nominal GDP: 10.4%
  • Quarterly GD Estimates: 6.9% (Q1FY27); 6.6% (Q2FY27); 6.7% (Q3FY27); and 6.9% (Q4FY27)
  • Major headwinds: Ongoing West Asia conflict; high headline inflation; a depreciating currency; and weaker-than-expected Capex; El-Nino on agriculture, among others
  • Inflation: WPI inflation (8.5%) and Retail inflation (4.9%)
  • Fiscal Deficit: 4.3% of GDP
  • CAD: Widen to 1.5% of GDP
Key Projections:
  • Nominal GDP: Also, Ind-Ra projected India’s nominal GDP growth at 10.4% for FY27, higher than 8.9% (in FY26).
  • Quarterly Estimates: Apart from projecting GDP growth for full FY27, the agency has estimated quarterly growth i.e. at 6.9% for the 1st Quarter (Q1: April-June) of FY27; 6.6% for Q2 (July-September); 6.7% for Q3 (October-December); and 6.9% for Q4 (January-March).
  • Inflation: The rating agency estimated India’s Wholesale Price Index (WPI) inflation to increase significantly to 8.5% (in FY27), while Consumer Price Index (CPI) inflation is projected to average at 4.9% for the current fiscal year.In addition, retail inflation is estimated to average at 4.9% in FY27 (over 2% in FY26), while for 3rd Quarter (Q3: October-December) of FY27, it is projected to increase to 5.9% before moderating to 5% in Q4 (January-March) of FY27.
About India Ratings and Research (Ind-Ra):
  • Chairman and Managing Director (CMD)- Rohit Karan Sawhney
  • Headquarters: Mumbai, Maharashtra
  • Established: 1995

  • Alignment: Further, this newly formed strategic partnership is expected to strengthen WAE’s contributions to the UN Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), particularly SDG-9 (Industry, Innovation and Infrastructure); SDG-12 (Responsible Consumption and Production); SDG-13 (Climate Action); and SDG-17 (Partnerships for the Goals).
  • About UN GCNI: Formed in 2000, it was officially registered as a non-profit society in 2003, to function as the Indian Country Network of the Global Compact, New York, the United States of America (USA).

10.1  48th Session of UNESCO WHC Held in Busan, South Korea: 25 New Sites, Including One from India Inscribed on the World Heritage Listp.178
Exam Hints:
  • What: 48th session of UNESCO WHC held
  • Venue: BEXCO (Busan, South Korea)
  • Chaired by: Lee Byong Hyun (from South Korea)
  • Sites Inscribed on WH List (2026): 25
  • Total UNESCO WH Sites: 1,273 (across 173 countries)
  • 1st-time Inscriptions: Comoros, Sao Tome and Principe, South Sudan
  • Key Declarations Adopted: Busan Declaration on World Heritage, USD 13 million SIDS strategy (2026–2034)
  • Next Host: Istanbul, Türkiye (2027).
Background:
  • These newly added sites comprise 19 cultural, 5 natural, and 1 mixed site, bringing the total number of UNESCO-recognised World Heritage (WH) sites to 1,273 across 173 countries.
Highlights of 48th Session of UNESCO WHC:
  • 1st-ever inscriptions: The session witnessed the 1st-ever WH inscriptions for 3 African nations: Comoros and Sao Tome and Principe and South Sudan.With the inclusion of these inscriptions, the number of state parties without heritage property decreased from 26 to 23.
  • Next Host: The 49th session of UNESCO WHC will be held from June 27 to July 7, 2027 in Istanbul, Türkiye under the presidency of Mustafa Yurdakul, its permanent delegate to UNESCO.

  • India’s Rank (2023): 57th in Market Distortions Performance Index (MDPI)

  • Key Parameters: 3 – Protection of Property Rights, Domestic Competition, and International Competition.
About the Report:
  • Aim: It tracks the country’s structural and pro-competitive reforms between 2010 and 2023.
  • Key Estimate: As per the report, the reductions in market distortions has lowered India’s projected 5-year Gross Domestic Product (GDP) per capita loss by percentage points.This number reflects a long-term measure which means approximately an additional 1% annual gain in GDP per capita Year-on-Year (Y-o-Y) as a result of the reforms it has undertaken in the study period of 2010 to 2023.
Market Reforms Impact:
  • Economic Cost: Remaining market distortions are estimated to impose an economic cost of around USD 173.6 billion over five years, equivalent to 4.2% of GDP.Foreign investment restrictions contribute the largest share of losses (USD 127.2 billion), followed by digital market competition policy challenges (USD 46.4 billion).

  • What? NITI Aayog released report “Reimagining Care: Strategies for Empowering Caregivers in Viksit Bharat@2047”
  • By Whom: Ashok Kumar Lahiri, Vice Chairperson, NITI Aayog
  • Where: New Delhi, Delhi
  • Objective: Develop a skilled caregiving workforce
  • Focus Areas: Proposes NPC & recommends creation of NCC
Background:
  • The report was released by Ashok Kumar Lahiri, Vice Chairperson, NITI Aayog, in the presence of Dr. R. Balasubramaniam, Member, NITI Aayog and Nidhi Chhibber, Chief Executive Officer (CEO), NITI Aayog, and senior officials from NITI Aayog.
About the Report:
  • Vision: The report envisions caregiving as a key pillar of India’s inclusive social and economic transformation, contributing to Viksit Bharat@2047 by recognising caregivers as skilled professionals and building an organised, professional, accessible and future-ready caregiving ecosystem.
  • Objectives: The report aims to improve quality of care, strengthen workforce welfare, enhance professional recognition, create employment opportunities and expand India’s role in the global caregiving economy.
  • Overview: apex public policy think tank and nodal agency of the Government of India, established in 2015 to replace the Planning Commission and promote economic development and cooperative federalism through a bottom-up approach.
  • Headquarters: New Delhi, Delhi

11.7  NITI Aayog Releases Report on India’s Manufacturing Sector, Identifies Four Key Sectors for Global Manufacturing Ambitionp.194
Exam Hints:
  • What: Release of a report titled “Key Sectors to Position India as a Global Manufacturing Hub”
  • Released by: Ashok Kumar Lahiri, Vice Chairperson, NITI Aayog
  • Key Objective: Data-driven assessment of India’s manufacturing sector
  • Sectors Examined: 12 manufacturing sectors
  • 4 High-Potential Sectors: Chemicals, Textiles, Telecom & Networking Equipment, and Solar Photovoltaic (PV) Manufacturing
  • Selection Parameters: Market potential, infrastructure, policy support, raw materials, technology readiness, employment potential and global value-chain position
  • Methodology: Four-phase assessment covering sector shortlisting, competitiveness assessment, global benchmarking and actionable recommendations.
Key Findings of the Report:
  • 4 High-potential Sectors: Out of the 12 key sectors, the report has identified chemicals, textiles, telecom and networking equipment, and Solar PV manufacturing as four high-potential sectors that have the potential to drive India’s manufacturing growth and strengthen its position in global value chains.
About 4 High-potential Sectors:
  • Solar PV: The report showed that India had 106 Giga-watt (GW) of installed solar capacity by March 2025 and needs to add around 174 GW to achieve its 280 GW target by 2030.India’s domestic PV market, estimated at nearly Rs 32,400 crore (USD 3.7 billion), is projected to grow at 17-20% CAGR between FY23 and FY30, supported by utility-scale solar, rooftop projects, open-access solar and demand linked to green hydrogen.The report emphasized greater focus on developing upstream capacity; enhanced R&D; specialised manufacturing equipment and skills; performance-linked support, integrated clean-tech clusters, among others.
