11.8  NITI Aayog Releases Report on Regulatory Framework for India’s Professional Services Sectorp.196
Exam Hints:
  • What: Release of NITI Aayog’s 3rd publication under its thematic series
  • Report title: “India’s Services Sector: Insights on Regulatory Regime in Professional Services”
  • Released by: Ashok Kumar Lahiri, Vice Chairperson of NITI Aayog
  • Purpose: Provides assessment of India’s regulatory framework for professional services
  • Exports: Nearly 1/4th (25%) of India’s services exports
  • Key Recommendations: Four-pronged strategy; sector-specific reforms.
Background:
  • The report was formally launched by Ashok Kumar Lahiri, Vice Chairperson of NITI Aayog, at a meeting of the High-Powered ‘Education to Employment and Enterprise’ Standing Committee held in New Delhi, Delhi.
Key Findings of the Report:
  • Aim: To identify challenges and policy measures to strengthen the professional services sector and unlock the potential of India’s skilled workforce.
  • India’s Services Sector: In 2024, India was the world’s 7th largest services exporter, accounting for 4.3% of global services exports.The report showed that professional and management consulting services accounted for approximately 20% of India’s total services exports in 2024-25.
  • Contribution to Services Exports: As per the report, Professional services account for nearly 1/4th (25%) of India’s total services exports.The report showed that professional services accounted for 19.7% of India’s total services exports (in 2023) and registered a Compound Annual Growth Rate (CAGR) of 185% between Financial Year 2014-15 (FY15) and FY25.

Exam Hints:
  • What: Release of India’s Trade Data for July 2026 and for April-July 2026-27
  • Released by: MoCI
  • India’s Overall Exports (Merchandise & Services): USD 80.14 billion (July 2026)USD 316.42 billion (April-July 2026-27)
  • India’s Overall Imports (Merchandise & Services): USD 95.16 billion in July 2026USD 365.85 billion (April-July 2026-27)
  • Trade Deficit: USD 15.03 billion (July 2026)USD 49.43 billion (April-July 2026-27)Sector-wise Growth: Iron ore exports (78.35%), petroleum products (+67.64%), electronic goods (+57.40%)Top 5 Export Destinations: Kenya; Singapore; Malaysia; China and the United States of America (USA).Top 5 Import Destinations: Oman; Taiwan; Russia; China; USA

  • India’s overall imports increased by 15.83% to USD 95.16 billion (in July 2026) from USD 82.16 billion in July 2025.

  • Merchandise Exports & Imports: The data showed that India’s merchandise exports increased by 19.63%, from USD 36.98 billion (in July 2025) to USD 44.24 billion (in July 2026), surpassing the previous record of USD 38.34 billion in July 2022.Also, during the same time period, India’s merchandise imports registered 17.52% Y-o-Y growth rate i.e. from USD 64.86 billion (in July 2025) to USD 76.22 billion (in July 2026).

11.10  NSO Releases PLFS Monthly Bulletin July 2026: Unemployment Rate Drops to 5.1%p.198
Exam Hints:
  • What: PLFS Monthly Bulletin July 2026 released
  • Released by: NSO, MoSPI
  • UR: Overall – 5.1%, Rural: 4.5%, Urban: 6.7%
  • LFPR: Overall – 55.4%, Rural: 58.0%, Urban: 50.4%
  • WPR: Overall – 52.5%
  • Approach Used: Current Weekly Status (CWS)
  • Series: 16th Monthly Bulletin.
Sample Size Details:
  • First Stage Unit (FSU): At the all-India level, a total number of 7,487 FSUs (4,120 rural sub- units and 3,367 urban sub-units) surveyed during the month July 2026.
  • Households: The number of households surveyed was 89,069 (49,054 in rural areas and 40,015 in urban areas) and number of persons surveyed was 3,71,021 (2,11,411 in rural areas and 1,59,610 in urban areas).
Labour Force Participation Rate (LFPR) in Current Weekly Status (CWS):
  • LFPR: LFPR for persons of age 15 years and above in the country stood at 55.4% in July 2026, from 54.4% in June 2026.LFPR in CWS for persons of age 15 years and above in rural and urban areas stood at 58.0% and 50.4%, respectively.
WPR (in %) in CWS for persons of age 15 years and above:
  • Definition: The estimate of workforce in the CWS provides the number of persons who worked for at least 1 hour on any day during the 7 days preceding the date of survey.
  • WPR: In July 2026, the WPR in CWS for persons of age 15 years and above at the all-India level was 52.5%, from 51.4% in June 2026.WPR in CWS for the same age was 55.4% in rural areas and 47.0% in urban areas.

11.11  NITI Aayog Report: 87 Million Indian Youth Are Not Studying, Working Or In Trainingp.200
Exam Hints:
  • What: Release of Report “Reimagining Skilling for Viksit Bharat@2047”
  • Released by: NITI Aayog
  • Key Estimate: 87 million youth categorisedas NEET
  • Age Group: 15 to 29 Years old
  • Data Source: 78th round of NSS conducted in 2021
  • Job Aligned with Education: Only 8.25% graduates.
Background:
  • The estimates presented in the report are based on data from the 78th round of the National Sample Survey (NSS) conducted in 2021.
Key Estimates:
  • Employability Gap: The report noted the weak link between formation and employment as it highlighted that only 8.25% of graduates are employed in roles aligned with their qualifications.
  • Fewer Vocational Subjects: The report highlighted that at present, fewer than 1 in 12 secondary schools offer vocational subjects, despite the National Education Policy (NEP) 2020 and National Curriculum Framework which emphasises on greater integration of vocational education and job skills from Class 6th.
  • Number of People Trained: As per the report, over 7.67 crore people trained since 2014-15.
  • Financial Support: Government of India (GoI) allocated Rs 34,000 crore in the Union Budget for Financial Year 2025-26 (FY26) towards skilling-related expenditure across various central ministries.
  • Headquarters: New Delhi, Delhi
  • Established in: 2015

About COSPAR Vikram Sarabhai Medal:
  • Background: The medal was established in 1990 in the honour of Vikram Sarabhai, who is considered father of Indian space programme and founder of India Space Research Organisation (ISRO), to recognise scientists from developing countries for their exemplary contributions to the field of space research.
  • Previous Indian Recipients: Previously, only 3 Indian scientists received the medal: Anil Bharadwaj, Director of Physical Research Laboratory (PRL) of ISRO (2024); famous physicist Gurbax Singh Lakhina (2014) and eminent scientist and Bharat Ratna Udupi Ramachandra (UR) Rao, former chairman of ISRO (1996).

13.3  Padma Bhushan Awardee Physicist Deepak Dhar wins Dirac Medal 2026p.216
Exam Hints:
  • What: Awarded 2026 Dirac Medal
  • Who: Professor Deepak Dhar
  • Field: Theoretical Physics and Statistical Physics
  • Awarded By: International Centre for Theoretical Physics (ICTP)
  • Significance: First Indian physicist to receive the Dirac Medal
  • Major Contribution: Self-organized criticality, stochastic processes, statistical mechanics
  • Associated Institution: Tata Institute of Fundamental Research (TIFR)
  • Known As: Pioneer of statistical physics research in India 2026 Dirac Medal citation: It recognised contributions to equilibrium and non-equilibrium statistical mechanics, optimisation, theoretical neuroscience, and artificial intelligence.
Background:
  • He became the first Indian physicist to receive the Dirac Medal
  • He shared the award with Bernard Derrida of France, Marc Mézard of Italy, and Haim Sompolinsky of Israel and the United States.
Awards:
  • Padma Bhushan: In 2023, Deepak Dhar, conferred with Padma Bhushan, third-highest civilian award of India from Government of India (GoI) in the field of social work for his contributions to Science & Engineering.
  • Boltzmann Medal (2022): Became the first Indian to be awarded the Boltzmann Medal, the highest international honor in statistical physics awarded by the International Union of Pure and Applied Physics (IUPAP).
About Dirac Medal:
  • Origin: Dirac Medal was established in 1985 by the ICTP and is awarded annually on 8th August, the birth anniversary of theoretical physicist Paul Dirac.
  • Rewards & Criteria: The award carries a prize of USD 5,000 and is not conferred on past Nobel Laureates, Fields Medalists, or Wolf Prize winners.

13.4  V.O. C Port Becomes India’s First Port to Receive ISO 50001:2018 Certification for Energy Managementp.217
Exam Hints:
  • What? India’s 1st port to receive ISO 50001: 2018 certification for EnMS
  • Name: V.O. Chidambaranar Port, Thoothukudi, TN
  • Certified by: Indian Register Quality Systems (IRQS)
  • Focus: Energy efficiency & sustainable port operations
  • Recognition: Scope-2 Emission-Free Port by Bureau Veritas.
Background:
  • The port was accredited by Bureau Veritas as a Scope-2 Emission-Free Port, with renewable sources meeting 93.89% of its electricity needs in the Financial Year (FY26).
About ISO 50001:
  • 2018 Certification:
  • Certification: An international standard for establishing, implementing, maintaining, and continually improving an EnMS.
  • Criteria: A port can achieve zero Scope-2 emissions when its renewable energy generation exceeds its total electricity consumption during a specified period.
  • Scope-2 Emissions: These are indirect greenhouse gas emissions associated with purchased electricity, steam, heating, or cooling.
  • About ISO: Established in 1947 and headquartered in Geneva, Switzerland, ISO develops international standards for quality, environmental, energy management, safety, and industrial processes.

About National Sports Awards:
  • Overview: National Sports Awards is the collective name for six sports honours of the India, namely the Maulana Abul Kalam Azad Trophy (1956–1957), Arjuna Award (1961), Dronacharya Award (1985), Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna (1991–1992), Dhyan Chand Award (2002), and Rashtriya Khel Protsahan Puraskar (2009), each instituted in their respective years.
  • 1st Recipients: Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna was first awarded to Indian Grandmaster (GM) and World Chess Champion Viswanathan Anand (Chess), the Maulana Abul Kalam Azad Trophy to University of Mumbai (Maharashtra), and the Rashtriya Khel Protsahan Puraskar (2009) jointly to Tata Steel and the Railways Sports Promotion Board.

13.10  Parichha Weir in Jhansi Recognised as World Heritage Irrigation Structure by ICIDp.227
Exam Hints:
  • What: Parichha Weir in Jhansi Recognised as WHIS
  • Recognised By: ICID
  • Date of Recognition: 13 August 2026
  • Location: Jhansi, Uttar Pradesh
  • River: Betwa River
  • Associated System: Betwa Canal System
  • Formal Award Presentation: France during the 26th ICID International Congress and 77th International Executive Council Meeting (October 2026)
Background:
  • The three other Indian irrigation structures Ashti Medium Project (Maharashtra), Chhapawara Dam (Rajasthan), and Pechiparai Dam (Tamil Nadu, TN) were selected for the WHIS Award 2026
  • The formal presentation of the WHIS Award 2026 will be held in October 2026 at Marseille, France, during the 26th ICID International Congress and 77th International Executive Council Meeting.
Highlights of Parichha Weir:
  • Features: The rubble-stone masonry Parichha Weir is 1,174 metres long and stands 16.8 metres (m) above the riverbed.
  • Irrigation Importance: The weir serves as the headworks of the Betwa Canal System, extending around 2,654 kilometre (km) and irrigating 4,28,360 hectares across five districts of UP and MP.
  • Historical Significance: Proposed by Captain Strachey in 1855, construction began in 1881, and the system was commissioned in 1886.
About WHIS Award:
  • Eligible Structures: It covers historic dams, water storage systems, barrages, waterwheels, canal networks and drainage structures with heritage importance.
  • Established: 1950

15.1  Astrobase Space Technologies Unveils India’s First Private 800 kN FFSC Rocket Engine ‘EVEREST’p.235
Exam Hints:
  • What: Launch of India’s 1st privately developed 800 kN FFSC rocket engine EVEREST
  • Developed by: Astrobase Space Technologies
  • Thrust: 800 kN
  • Test Facility: Anantapur, Andhra Pradesh
  • Support: Technology Adoption Fund
  • Purpose: Reusable medium-lift launch vehicles and advanced orbital missions.
About EVEREST Rocket Engine:
  • Propulsion: Liquid Oxygen (LOX)–methane engine delivering 800 kN thrust, 50%–110% throttle, approximately 340 seconds specific impulse, enabling precise control, multiple restarts, reusable medium-lift and orbital missions.
  • Development: Astrobase completed sub-scale and turbopump tests in 2025–26, plans around 20 engines, and targets full-scale hot-fire testing in 2026.Its facilities include a 46,000 square feet (sq ft) Bengaluru facility, Karnataka and a 21.5-acre Anantapur test site, Andhra Pradesh (AP) with the first launch targeted for December 2028
  • About Astrobase Space Technologies: Founded in 2024 and headquartered in Bengaluru, Karnataka, it is an Indian deep-tech aerospace startup, led by co-founder and Chief Executive Officer (CEO) Neeraj Khandelwal.It was selected under the Technology Adoption Fund (TAF) on in June 2026 for indigenous development of its 800 kN LOX–methane engine.

15.2  Vietnam’s VinSpace Signs SpaceX Launch Deal for 2027 Transporter Rideshare Missionp.236
Exam Hints:
  • What: Signing of satellite launch agreement
  • Entities: VinSpace (unit of Vingroup, Vietnam) & SpaceX (USA)
  • Launch: Scheduled for 2027
  • Mission Type: Transporter rideshare mission
  • Objective: Develop Vietnam’s space capabilities
About VinSpace–SpaceX Satellite Launch Deal:
  • Objective: The project aims to build indigenous space capabilities, enhance satellite engineering expertise, and create a foundation for future commercial applications and international collaborations in the space sector.
  • Features: The agreement utilizes SpaceX’s Transporter rideshare model, allowing multiple payloads on a single Falcon 9 launch, reducing costs while enabling efficient access to orbit for small satellite developers.
  • Implementation: VinSpace will manage the complete satellite lifecycle, including design, assembly, integration, testing, launch coordination and in-orbit operations, ensuring full control over mission execution and data services.
  • About VinSpace: VinSpace, founded on November 3, 2025, and headquartered in Hanoi, Vietnam, is focused on developing comprehensive satellite capabilities across research, manufacturing, testing, launch, and operations.The company is led by Vu Trong Thu, who serves as its Chief Executive Officer (CEO) and Director General (DG).

  • Weight: ~250 kg
  • Endurance & Range: 30 hours; 3,000km
  • Key Capabilities: Precision strikes; swarm operations; real-time ISR
  • Testbed Role: First flying demonstrator for FWD Supreme family.
About FWD Supreme Lite:

  • Key Objectives: The performance objectives of the programme divided into two phases:Phase-I: aims to achieve maximum Mach 0.9 speed; nearly Mach 0.5 cruise speed; 7,00 to 1,000 kilometer (km) operational range depending on missile profile, among others.Phase-II: supersonic performance targets (i.e. to reach Mach 2 speed); multi-aircraft autonomous teaming, among others.

  • Orbit Duration: every 17.75 days.
About GJ 523b:
  • Discovery: It was initially discovered by National Aeronautics and Space Administration (NASA)’s Transiting Exoplanet Survey Satellite (TESS) but, its existence was confirmed by Max Krof from Wisconsin Center for Origins Research (WiCOR), using ground-based instruments, including the WIYN 3.5meter (m) Telescope at Kitt Peak National Observatory in Arizona, the United States of America (USA).
  • Size and Mass: The newly discovered exoplanet has a radius of nearly 2.55 times larger than Earth and a density of about 126.82 grams (gms) per cubic inch.
  • Orbit: It orbits a mid-sized, orange K-dwarf star ( 87 light years away from the Earth) every 17.75 days in a unique, highly tilted polar orbit.

15.9  US Navy Unveils ‘AIM-424 Malice’ Long-Range Air-to-Air Missile With 463 km Rangep.240
Exam Hints:
  • What: AIM-424 Malice long-range air-to-air missile
  • By Whom: US Navy
  • Developer: Raytheon (RTX Corporation)
  • Type: Long-Range Air-to-Air Missile
  • Range: >250 nautical miles (~463 km).
Background:
  • The missile is designed to strengthen long-range aerial combat and naval defence capabilities.
About AIM-424 Malice Missile:
  • Technology: It uses a two-stage solid-propellant rocket motor enabling high-speed, long-distance engagement with sustained energy for terminal manoeuvres against moving aerial targets.
  • Guidance: The system integrates inertial navigation, satellite and datalink updates, and advanced terminal seekers combining radar and infrared sensors for high accuracy and resistance to jamming.
  • Compatibility: The missile is designed for internal carriage in stealth aircraft such as the F-35 Lightning II and F-22 Raptor, and external carriage on the F/A-18 Super Hornet.
  • Role: It is intended to enable a ‘first look, first shot, first kill’ advantage for fleet air defence by targeting high-value airborne assets such as AWACS (Airborne Warning and Control System), aerial refuelling tankers, and enemy bombers before they launch attacks.

  • Purpose: Indigenous propulsion for UAVs, drone interceptors, missile systems.
About Indigenous Gas Turbine Engines (NJ Series):
  • Engines: Gas turbine engines operate on the Brayton cycle, a thermodynamic cycle widely used in jet propulsion and power generation, where air is compressed, mixed with fuel, combusted, and expanded through a turbine to produce thrust or shaft power.
  • Ecosystem: The programme aims to create a domestic ecosystem involving Indian industries and aerospace startups for large-scale manufacturing and integration into defence platforms.
  • Development: NJ-100 engine builds upon earlier NJ-05 demonstrator data, indicating progressive scaling within the same engine family, showcasing India’s growing expertise in miniaturised gas turbine technologies.
  • Establishment: NAL is India’s only government-run civilian aerospace research and development laboratory , established in 1959 by the CSIR, and operates under the Ministry of Science and Technology (MoS&T).
  • Director: Abhay A.Pashilkar
  • Headquarters: Bengaluru, Karnataka

16.8  Indian defence Start-up D-Propulse Successfully Demonstrated India’s first indigenous 5 kN air-breathing RDEp.248
Exam Hints:
  • What: Successful demonstration of India’s 1st indigenous 5 kN air-breathing RDE
  • Demonstrated by: D-Propulse Aerospace Pvt. Ltd.
  • Key Milestone Achieved: Validation of TRL-5
  • Test Conducted at: DRDO test facility (Hyderabad, Telangana)
  • Incubated at: IIT Madras (Chennai, TN)
Key Details of 5 kN Air-breathing RDE:
  • Technology: Unlike traditional jet engines, RDEs use pressure-gain combustion which improves thermodynamic efficiency and eliminates moving parts inside the combustor, which results in a simple engine architecture.
  • Significance: The new RDE offers 15-25% higher thermodynamic efficiency compared to conventional air-breathing propulsion systems and relies on precision machining rather than turbine complex turbine assemblies expected to lower production costs and reduce manufacturing timelines.
  • Schedule for: The newly demonstrated RDE, incubated at Indian Institute of Technology, Madras (IIT-M) in Chennai, Tamil Nadu (TN), is scheduled to be launched by December 2027.
  • About D-Propulse: Founded in July 2025 by V Ramanujachari and Saurav Jha, D-Propulse, with headquarters in New Delhi (Delhi), builds next-generation RDEs for high-supersonic and hypersonic flight.

  • Name Origin: Derived from Mangrol (Surat, Gujarat) and legacy of earlier INS Mangrol
  • Local Content: Nearly 80%
  • Propulsion: Diesel Engine–Waterjet system
  • Key Systems: Lightweight torpedoes + ASW rockets + SONAR (Sound Navigation and Ranging)
  • Earlier Ships: INS Mahe, INS Malvan.
About INS Mangrol:
  • Name: The vessel name ‘Mangrol’ draws its inspiration from the historic, strategically significant coastal township of Mangrol located in Surat district, Gujarat.The naming honours the legacy of the earlier INS Mangrol, an Indian Naval Minesweeper that remained in service until 2004.
  • Local Content: It boasts nearly 80% indigenous content, marking another milestone in indigenous warship construction supporting the Government of India’s (GoI)’s ‘Aatmanirbhar Bharat(Self-Reliance)’ initiative.
About Anti-Submarine Warfare Shallow Water Crafts (ASW-SWCs):
  • Background: Two contracts for 16 ASW-SWCs, 8 each for CSL and Garden Reach Shipbuilders & Engineers (GRSE), were signed in April 2019.
  • Replaced: These vessels are being designed to replace the ageing Abhay-class corvettes of the IN.
  • Commissioned ASW-SWCs: So far, two Mahe-class ASW-SWCs: INS Mahe (October 2025) and INS Malvan (in July 2026) commissioned into the IN.

  • Established: 1972

17.1  Ajinkya Rahane Retires from International Cricket After Distinguished 12-Year India Careerp.261
Exam Hints:
  • What: Announcement of retirement from international cricket
  • Who: Ajinkya Rahane, Former India Test vice-captain and stand-in Test captain
  • Career Span: International Career: 2011–2023
  • Total International: 195 matches; 8,414 runs; 15 centuries; 51 half-centuries.
  • Captaincy Record: 6 Tests (4 wins, 2 draws)– undefeated as captain; one of few Indian captains with 5+ Tests without defeat.
About Ajinkya Rahane:
  • Tests: Played 85 Test matches, scoring 5,077 runs at an average of 38.46, with 12 centuries and 26 half-centuries, becoming one of India’s most successful overseas middle-order batters.
  • Limited-Overs: Played 90 ODIs, scoring 2,962 runs with 3 centuries and 24 half-centuries, represented India at the 2015 International Cricket Council (ICC) Men’s Cricket World Cup, and featured in 20 T20Is, scoring 375 runs after making his debut against England in 2011.
  • Achievements: Scored 8 of his 12 Test centuries overseas (England, Australia, New Zealand, Sri Lanka and the West Indies) and was India’s highest first-innings scorer in both the 2021 and 2023 ICC World Test Championship (WTC) Finals.
  • Domestic: Captained Mumbai to its record-extending 42nd Ranji Trophy title in 2023–24 and, played in 212 IPL matches, scored 5,367 runs at a strike rate of 125.60, including 2 centuries and 35 half-centuries, while representing six franchises and reinventing his batting style later in his career.

  • The tournament was held from 15 to 23 August 2026 at Kanizsa Aréna, Nagykanizsa, in a 9-round Swiss-system format.
About Pranesh M:
  • Origin: Pranesh M progressed through the titles of International Chess Federation (FIDE) Master (FM) in 2019, International Master (IM) in 2020, and GM in January 2023 at age 16.
  • Achievements: Following his GM title, he secured joint first at the Silver Lake Open in Serbia (2023) and Tsaghkadzor Open in Armenia (2023), and won the Challengers section of the Chennai Grand Masters in 2025 with 6.5/9.
  • Ranking: Pranesh’s FIDE rating rose from 2633 in March 2026 to 2673 by August 2026, placing him 44th in the world rankings.

18.4  Record-Breaking Mountaineer Nirmal Purja Passed Awayp.277
Exam Hints:
  • What: Renowned Mountaineer Passed Away
  • Who: Nirmal Purja
  • Cause of Death: Avalanche on Broad Peak, Pakistan
  • Historic Record: Climbed all 14 peaks above 8,000 m in 189 days (2019)
  • Major Achievement: First winter ascent of K2 with an all-Nepali team (2021)
  • Award: Member of the Order of the British Empire.
Background:
  • He was born on 25 July 1983 in Myagdi, Gandaki Province, Nepal.
About Nirmal Purja:
  • Early Career: He joined the Brigade of Gurkhas in 2003, became the first Gurkha inducted into the Royal Navy’s Special Boat Service (SBS) in 2009, served as a cold-weather warfare specialist, and resigned in 2018 to pursue high-altitude mountaineering full-time.
  • Achievement: In 2019, he completed Project Possible 14/7 by climbing all 14 eight-thousanders (peaks above 8,000 metres) in 6 months and 6 days using bottled oxygen, breaking the previous record by over seven years.He became the first person to climb Mount Everest, Lhotse and Makalu within 48 hours and achieved the first winter ascent of K2 (Karakoram Range) with Nepali climbers in 2021, reaching the summit without supplemental oxygen.
About Broad Peak:
  • Elevation: Stands at 8,051 m (26,414 ft), making it the 12th-highest mountain in the world.
  • First Ascent: First successfully climbed on 9 June 1957 by an Austrian expedition led by Marcus Schmuck.

20.4  World Wide Web Day 2026 – August 1p.290
Exam Hints:
  • What: World Wide Web Day 2026
  • When: August 1, 2026
  • Inventor of WWW: Tim Berners-Lee (1989)
  • First Web Server & Browser: httpd and WorldWideWeb (1990)
  • First Website: info.cern.ch (6 August 1991)
  • HTTP Development: Developed with Robert Cailliau; released in 1992.
Background:
  • Origin: The day commemorates the anniversary of the development of the WWW by Tim Berners-Lee.
  • Recognition: It honours the contributions of individuals who helped shape the Web into its present advanced and organised form.
History:
  • WWW Invention: Sir Tim Berners-Lee invented the WWW in 1989 at CERN (European Organisation for Nuclear Research), Geneva, Switzerland.He developed the first web server (httpd) and browser (WorldWideWeb) in 1990, and launched the world’s first website, info.cern.ch, on 6 August 1991.
  • HTTP Development: Along with Robert Cailliau, he developed the Hypertext Transfer Protocol (HTTP), which was released in 1992.
Evolution of Web Technology:
  • Web 1.0 (1990–2000): Introduced static, read-only web pages focused on information sharing.Web 2.0 (2000–2010): Enabled interactive websites, social media, blogs, user-generated content and online shopping platforms.Web 3.0 (2010–Present): Focuses on Artificial Intelligence (AI), semantic web, cloud technologies, personalisation and decentralised systems.

20.5  Indian Organ Donation Day 2026 – August 3p.291
Exam Hints:
  • What: Indian Organ Donation Day 2026
  • When: 3 August 2026
  • First Observed: 2010
  • Nodal Organisation: NOTTO, MoHFW

  • 2026 Event: Campaign: ‘Jug Jug Jiyo’ launched by MoS Anupriya PatelDigital Initiatives: e-Pratyaropan platform & NOTTO Mobile AppGuidelines Released: National Brain Stem Death Certification Guidelines & National Guidelines on Swap TransplantationBest State Award: Tamil Nadu.
Background:
  • 2026 marks the 16th IODD Observance in India.
Background:
  • First Observance: National Organ and Tissue Transplant Organization (NOTTO), under the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare (MoHFW), began observing IODD in 2010, and it was celebrated annually on 27 November until 2022.
  • Date Change: In 2023, the observance was shifted to 3 August to commemorate the anniversary of India’s first successful heart transplant.
About Organ Donation:
  • Legal Framework: Transplantation of Human Organs Act (THOA), 1994 provided legal recognition to brain death and regulated living and deceased donor organ transplantation, and was amended in 2011 as the THOTA, 2011 to include tissue donation.
  • Registry: Out of 804 registered transplant hospitals, 217 hospitals failed to report data on the National Organ and Tissue Transplant Registry Portal in 2025.As of 3 March 2026, India’s organ transplant waitlist stood at 89,839, with 20,019 transplants performed in 2025.

20.6  World Breastfeeding Week 2026 – August 1-7p.293
Exam Hints:
  • What: World Breastfeeding Week 2026
  • When: 1–7 August, 2026
  • Theme 2026: “Breastfeeding for a sustainable start in life: strengthen what works”
  • First Observed: 1992
  • Origin: Innocenti Declaration, 1990
  • Coordinated by: World Alliance for Breastfeeding Action
  • Supported by: WHO & UNICEF.
About Breastfeeding:
  • WHO–UNICEF Update: WHO and United Nations Children’s Fund (UNICEF) reported that exclusive breastfeeding during the first six months increased from 37% (2012) to over 47%, while breastfeeding up to two years rose from 38% to 50%.They noted that scaling up breastfeeding support could prevent nearly 400,000 child deaths and 140,000 maternal deaths annually, with every USD 1 invested generating an estimated USD 59 in economic returns.
Breastfeeding Status in India:
  • Current Scenario: India’s exclusive breastfeeding rate declined from 63.7% (National Family Health Survey (NFHS)-5: 2019–21) to 55.8% (NFHS-6: 2023–24), while early initiation within one hour improved from 41.8% to 50.1%.
2026 Event:
  • Webinar: Global Breastfeeding Collective (GBC), led by the UNICEF and the WHO, is hosting a two-session virtual webinar (4–5 August 2026) titled “Strengthening What Works: New Evidence to Accelerate Investment in Breastfeeding” to mark World Breastfeeding Week 2026.
About World Alliance for Breastfeeding Action (WABA):
  • Executive Director (ED): Amal Omer Salim
  • Established: 1991

20.8  Hiroshima Day 2026 – August 6p.295
Exam Hints:
  • What Day: Hiroshima Day 2026
  • Date: 6 August (Annually)
  • Significance: Commemorates victims of the 1945 atomic bombing
  • 2026 Observance: 81st anniversary of the Hiroshima atomic bombing
  • Location: Hiroshima, Japan
  • Atomic Bomb: Little Boy (Uranium-235)
  • Aircraft: USAAF B-29 Enola Gay.
Background:
  • 2026 marks the 81st anniversary of the atomic bombing of Hiroshima.
About WWII Atomic Bombings:
  • Bombing: United States Army Air Forces (USAAF B-29) Superfortress ‘Enola Gay’ dropped the “Little Boy” atomic bomb on Hiroshima on 6 August 1945.Uranium-235 (U-235) bomb had a force of 15,000 tons of Trinitrotoluene (TNT) and was detonated at 1,800 feet.It weighed approximately 9,700 pounds (4,400 kilogram, kg) and measured 10 feet (3.05 metres, m) in length with a 28-inch diameter.
  • Impact: Hiroshima bombing destroyed 13 square kilometres (sq km) and caused around 140,000 deaths by the end of 1945.On 9 August 1945, the B-29 Bock’s Car dropped the second atomic bomb, “Fat Man,” on Nagasaki, killing around 40,000 people.
  • Aftermath: Following the bombings, Japan surrendered on 15 August 1945 and signed the Instrument of Surrender (IoS) on 2 September 1945 aboard the USS Missouri.

Capital: Tokyo
Currency: Japanese Yen (JPY)

  • 1st Observance Day: 2018

  • Prepared under: GoI–Global Environment Facility (GEF)–United Nations Development Programme (UNDP) initiative.
Background:
  • Origin: International Clouded Leopard Day was launched by the Aspinall Foundation in 2018 to promote awareness and conservation of clouded leopards.
  • First Observance: The day was first observed on August 4, 2018 at Howletts Wild Animal Park, United Kingdom (UK).
About Clouded Leopard:
  • IUCN Status: Since 2008, the species listed as “Vulnerable” on the International Union for Conservation of Nature (IUCN) Red list.
  • Key Threats: Habitat loss is one of the biggest threats for clouded leopards as 34% of mainland clouded leopard habitat was lost between 2000 and 2018 due to deforestation.Some of the other key threats are: poaching and illegal trade of species skin and bones; human-wildlife conflict, among others.
2026 Event:
  • Priority Conservation Landscapes: The plan identifies 14 priority conservation landscapes across the NE region and suggests landscape-level habitat restoration and corridor conservation to strengthen connectivity between forest habitats.

Major Handloom Sector Schemes in India:
  • Raw Material Supply Scheme (RMSS): It was originally introduced as the Mill Gate Price Scheme in 1992 and later renamed the Yarn Supply Scheme, was restructured for 2021–22 to 2025–26 as a Central Sector Scheme (CSS) of Ministry of Textiles (MoT) providing eligible handloom weavers with a 15% price subsidy on natural yarns and 100% freight reimbursement.
  • e-Pehchan portal: Launched in 2025, enables new workers to register and existing workers to update details and download their Pehchan/e-Pehchan cards.
  • Small Cluster Development Programme (SCDP): Provides up to Rs. 2 crore per handloom cluster, with 357 clusters sanctioned during 2021–22 to June 2026–27 and Rs.94 crore released in 2025–26 for key interventions.

  • Celebration: MoT celebrated the 12th National Handloom Day on 7 August 2026 at the Rashtrapati Bhavan Cultural Centre (RBCC), New Delhi, Delhi.

20.12  Quit India Movement Day 2026- August 8p.301
Exam Hints:
  • What: Quit India Movement Day/August Kranti Diwas 2026
  • When: August 8, 2026
  • Anniversary: 84th
  • Movement: Launched in 1942, led by Mahatma Gandhi
  • Proposed by: Pandit Jawahar Lal Nehru
  • Coinage: Yusuf Meherally coined the phrase “Quit India”
Background:
  • August 8 2026 marks the 84th anniversary of the launch of Quit India Movement Day.
About Quit India Movement:
  • Background: The failure of the ‘Cripps Mission’ in April 1942 to secure Indian cooperation during World War II (WWII) led to the launch of the Quit India Movement.
  • Movement: Quit India Movement, known as the August Kranti, August Movement, or Bharat Chodo Andolan, was launched after Mahatma Gandhi gave the historic “Do or Die” (Karo ya Maro) call at Gowalia Tank Maidan (now August Kranti Maidan), Bombay (now Mumbai), on 8 August 1942.The movement gained momentum on 9 August 1942, following the arrest of Gandhi and other Congress leaders by the British.
  • Leadership: Aruna Asaf Ali, known as the “Heroine of 1942”, “Queen of the August Revolution” and “Grand Old Lady of the Independence Movement”, hoisted the Indian flag at Gowalia Tank Maidan and was later awarded the Padma Vibhushan (1992) and the Bharat Ratna (posthumously, 1997).

IMPORTANT DAYS
20.13  Nagasaki Day 2026 – August 9p.301
Exam Hints:
  • What Day: Nagasaki Day 2026
  • When: 9 August, 2026
  • Anniversary: 81st Anniversary of Nagasaki Atomic Bombing
  • Year of Bombing: 1945
  • Bomb: “Fat Man”
  • Aircraft: B-29 bomber Bockscar.
Background:
  • 2026 marks the 81st anniversary of the atomic bombing of Nagasaki.
Nagasaki Atomic Bombing (1945):
  • Bombing Details: On 9 August 1945, the United States Army Air Forces (USAAF) B-29 bomber ‘Bockscar’ dropped the second atomic bomb, ‘Fat Man’, on Nagasaki, Japan, which was detonated at an altitude of around 1,650 feet after being released from approximately 29,000 feet.
  • Fat Man: plutonium-based implosion-type atomic bomb with an explosive force of 21,000 tons of TNT (Trinitrotoluene), weighing over 10,800 pounds (approx. 4,670 kilogram, kg), with a length of 10.8 feet and a diameter of 60 inches.
Impact & Aftermath:
  • Destruction: The bombing destroyed around 3 square miles of Nagasaki and caused approximately 140,000 deaths by the end of 1945.
  • Hiroshima Attack: Nagasaki bombing followed the Hiroshima bombing on 6 August 1945, where the first atomic bomb “Little Boy” was dropped.
2026 Events:
  • Ceremony: The 81st Nagasaki Peace Memorial Ceremony was held on 9 August 2026 at Nagasaki Peace Park, Japan.

20.14  International Day of the World’s Indigenous Peoples 2026 – August 9p.302
Exam Hints:
  • What Day: International Day of the World’s Indigenous Peoples or World Tribal Day 2026
  • When: 9 August, 2026
  • Organised By: United Nations (UN)
  • 2026 Theme: “Honouring Indigenous Midwives: Safeguarding Life and Well-being”
  • Purpose: Protect indigenous peoples’ rights and promote their cultures, traditions and knowledge.
Background:
  • UN Declaration: United Nations General Assembly (UNGA) adopted Resolution A/RES/49/214 on 23 December 1994 and proclaimed 9 August as the International Day of the World’s Indigenous Peoples.
  • First Observance: The first International Day of the World’s Indigenous Peoples was observed on 9 August 1995.
  • Significance: The date commemorates the first meeting of the UN Working Group on Indigenous Populations (WGIP) held in 1982 in Geneva, Switzerland.
UN Initiatives for Indigenous Peoples:
  • International Decades: UN declared 1995–2004 as the International Decade of the World’s Indigenous People and 2005–2014 as the Second International Decade of the World’s Indigenous People.
  • Language Protection: 2019 was observed as the International Year of Indigenous Languages, while 2022–2032 is the International Decade of Indigenous Languages.
Facts about Indigenous Peoples:
  • Diversity: Indigenous communities speak more than 7,000 languages, represent around 5,000 cultures worldwide, and over 86% of indigenous people work in the informal economy.
United Nations General Assembly (UNGA) - Static GK:
President: Annalena Baerbock
Headquarters: New York City, United States of America (USA)
Established: 1945

20.15  World Lion Day 2026 – August 10p.303
Exam Hints:
  • What Day: World Lion Day 2026
  • Observed on: 10 August 2026
  • First Observance: 2013
  • Purpose: To protect and conserve lion populations
  • Species: Lion (Panthera leo)
  • India’s Lion Species: Asiatic Lion (Panthera leo persica)
  • Habitat in India: Gir Forest, Gujarat
  • 2026 Celebration: Union Minister Bhupender Yadav, MoEFCC, noted the Asiatic lion population rise from 523 (2015) to 891 (2025)
Background:
  • 10 August 2026 marks the 14th observance of the World Lion Day.
Background:
  • Initiation: World Lion Day was started in 2013 by Big Cat Rescue (BCR), a wildlife sanctuary in Florida, United States of America (USA), dedicated to lion conservation.
About Lions:
  • Population: Around 23,000 to 39,000 lions remain in the wild, with Tanzania (East Africa) having the highest wild lion population of approximately 17,200 lions.
Asiatic Lion Conservation Success (2026):
  • Celebration: Union Minister, Bhupender Yadav, Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change (MoEFCC) highlighted the rise of the Asiatic lion population from 523 (2015) to 891 (2025) due to conservation efforts, reaffirming India’s commitment towards protecting wildlife and biodiversity.
  • Project Lion Progress: Launched by Prime Minister (PM) Narendra Modi in 15 August 2020, the lion habitat range expanded from around 30,000 square kilometer (sq. km) in 2020 to nearly 35,000 sq. km, supported by habitat improvement, disease monitoring, scientific tracking and community participation.
About International Big Cat Alliance (IBCA):
  • Launched: 2023

20.16  World Biofuel Day 2026 – August 10p.304
Exam Hints:
  • What Day: World Biofuel Day 2026
  • Date: 10 August (Annually)
  • Observed by India: MoPNG since 2015
  • Commemorates: Rudolf Diesel’s demonstration of a peanut-oil-powered diesel engine in 1893
  • Purpose: Promote biofuels as renewable alternatives to fossil fuels and reduce GHG emissions.
Background:
  • Initiative: Since 2015, the Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas (MoPNG), Government of India (GoI) observing World Biofuel Day to promote the use of blended biofuels in India.
  • Significance: The day commemorates Rudolf Christian Karl Diesel, who demonstrated his diesel engine powered by peanut oil (biofuel) on 10 August 1893 in Augsburg, Germany.
India’s Biofuel Progress:
  • Ethanol production: India’s ethanol production capacity increased to about 2,000 crore litres in 2026.Ethanol Blending: India achieved 20% ethanol blending in petrol in 2025–26.Biodiesel Target: India aims for 5% biodiesel blending by 2030.
Government Initiatives:
  • Pradhan Mantri JI-VAN Yojana: Pradhan Mantri JI-VAN (Jaiv Indhan–Vatavaran Anukool fasal awashesh Nivaran) Yojana was launched on 7 March 2019 to provide financial assistance for 2G bio-ethanol projects.
  • Global Biofuel Alliance (GBA): Launched during India’s G20 Presidency in September 2023, the alliance promotes global cooperation in biofuel development.
Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas (MoPNG) - Static GK:
Union Minister: Hardeep Singh Puri (Rajya Sabha- Uttar Pradesh, UP)
Minister of State (MoS): Suresh Gopi (Constituency – Thrissur, Kerala)

20.17  World Steelpan Day 2026 – August 11p.305
Exam Hints:
  • What day: World Steelpan Day 2026
  • Date: 11 August (Annually)
  • Purpose: To recognise the cultural and historical significance of steelpan
  • First Observed: 11 August 2023
  • Origin Country: Trinidad and Tobago.
Background:
  • 11 August 2026 marks the 4th edition of World Steelpan Day.
Background:
  • Adoption: On 24 July 2023, the United Nations General Assembly (UNGA) adopted Resolution A/RES/77/316, officially declaring 11 August as World Steelpan Day.
  • First Observance: The first World Steelpan Day was observed on 11 August 2023.
About Steelpan:
  • Origin: Steelpan was created in T&T in the early 1930s and is considered the only acoustic musical instrument invented in the 20th century.
  • Instrument Type: Steelpan, known as pan or steel drum, is a percussion instrument belonging to the idiophone family.
  • National Instrument: Government of T&T declared steelpan as its national musical instrument in 1992.
Recognition:
  • UNESCO Recognition: In 2019, Port of Spain (PoS), T&T, was designated as a UNESCO Creative City of Music for promoting creativity and sustainable urban development through steelpan.
  • Panorama Competition: Panorama, the first national steelpan competition in T&T, was launched in 1963.
  • Headquarters: Paris, France
  • Established: 1945

20.19  World Elephant Day 2026 – August 12p.307
Exam Hints:
  • What: World Elephant Day 2026
  • When: 12 August 2026
  • First Observed: 12 August 2012
  • Purpose: To raise awareness about the conservation and protection of elephants
  • Global Significance: India hosts nearly 60% of the world’s wild Asian elephant population
  • Conservation Network: 33 Elephant Reserves and 150 identified elephant corridors.
Background:
  • First Observance: The first World Elephant Day was observed on 12 August 2012 by Patricia Sims and the Elephant Reintroduction Foundation.
About Elephant:
  • Largest Land Animal: Elephants are the largest land animals on Earth and are known for their intelligence, social behaviour, and strong family bonds.Elephants have a 22-month gestation period and usually give birth to one calf every 4–5 years.
Population:
  • African Savanna Elephant: Around 415,000 individuals remain, in East and Southern Africa.African Forest Elephant: Estimated population is below 50,000.
Elephants in India:
  • Population: India hosts nearly 60% of the world’s wild Asian elephant population, with the Synchronous All India Population Estimation of Elephants (SAIEE) 2021–25 estimating 22,446 wild elephants.Elephants are protected through 33 Elephant Reserves and 150 identified corridors across the country.
  • National Heritage Animal: Government of India (GoI) declared the elephant as India’s National Heritage Animal in 2010.
International Union for Conservation of Nature (IUCN) - Static GK:
President: Razan Khalifa AI Mubarak
Headquarters: Gland, Switzerland
Established: 1948

20.20  International Left Handers Day 2026 – August 13p.309
Exam Hints:
  • What: International Left-Handers Day 2026
  • Observed on: 13 August (Annually)
  • Purpose: To celebrate left-handed people and raise awareness
  • First Observed: 13 August 1976
  • Global Recognition: Launched by the Left-Handers Club in 1992.
Background:
  • Concept: The day was conceptualised by Dean Robert Campbell in 1976 to highlight the uniqueness, advantages and challenges of left-handed people.
  • Recognition: Left-Handers Club officially launched International Left-Handers Day on 13 August 1992 to increase awareness about left-handedness.
Scientific Insights:
  • Gene Link: A rare gene variant TUBB4B is associated with left-handedness and may influence brain asymmetry and hand dominance.
  • Genetic Frequency: TUBB4B variants are 2.7 times more common in left-handed people, though they account for only a small percentage of cases.
  • Brain Function: Left-handedness is linked with differences in brain hemisphere functions, where the right hemisphere often controls the dominant hand.
Key Facts:
  • Southpaw Origin: The term Southpaw refers to left-handed people and originated from baseball pitchers in the United States of America (USA).
  • Phobia: Sinistrophobia is the fear of left-handedness or things located on the left side of the body.

20.21  World Organ Donation Day 2026 – August 13p.309
Exam Hints:
  • What: World Organ Donation Day 2026
  • When: 13 August 2026
  • Commemoration: First Transplant in 1954 by American Surgeon Dr. Joseph E. Murray
  • Nobel Prize: Awarded in 1990 in Physiology or Medicine to Joseph E. Murray and E. Donnall Thomas.
  • Observance in India: 3 August 2026, organised by NOTTO under MoHFW
Background:
  • Observance in India: Indian Organ Donation Day (IODD) is observed annually across India on August 3 by the National Organ and Tissue Transplant Organization (NOTTO) under the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare (MoHFW) to commemorate India’s first successful deceased-donor organ transplant, the first heart transplant.August 3, 2026 marks the 16th observance of IODD.
Types of Organ Donation:
  • Living: A living person can donate a kidney, part of the liver, lung, or pancreas without harming their health.
  • Impact: One donor can save up to 8 lives through organ donation and improve 75+ lives through tissue and cornea donation by donating multiple organs.
  • Statistics: According to a January 2026 United Network for Organ Sharing (UNOS) report, the United States of America (USA) recorded more than 49,000 organ transplants in 2025, marking the fifth consecutive annual record, while deceased organ donations declined by 2.5% to 16,550 donors, highlighting the continuing gap between transplant demand and organ availability.
United Network for Organ Sharing (UNOS) - Static GK:
Chief Executive Officer (CEO): Mark Johnson (Interim)
Headquarters: Richmond, Virginia, United States of America (USA)
Established: 1984

20.22  Partition Horrors Remembrance Day 2026 – August 14p.310
Exam Hints:
  • What: Partition Horrors Remembrance Day 2026
  • When: August 14, 2026
  • Edition: 6th
  • Purpose: To honour victims and remember the suffering caused by the 1947 Partition of British India
  • Declared by: Prime Minister Narendra Modi in 2021
  • First Observance: August 14, 2021.
Background:
  • August 14, 2026 marks the 6th annual commemoration of Partition Horrors Remembrance Day in India.
Background:
  • Official Designation: In 2021, Prime Minister (PM) Narendra Modi officially designated August 14 as Partition Horrors Remembrance Day to commemorate the victims of Partition and highlight the impact of the tragedy on millions of people.
  • First Observance: The first Partition Horrors Remembrance Day was observed on August 14, 2021.
Legacy of Partition:
  • Nation Formation: Partition resulted in the creation of India and Pakistan, triggering large-scale migration across newly formed borders.
  • Boundary Division: Partition plan was announced on 3 June 1947 by Lord Louis Mountbatten, while the boundaries were drawn by the Radcliffe Boundary Commission headed by Cyril Radcliffe.
  • Migration Scale: Around 6 million non-Muslims migrated from West Pakistan, while approximately 6.5 million Muslims moved from Indian regions like Punjab and Delhi towards West Pakistan.

About International Humanitarian Law (IHL):
  • Key Facts: By May 2026, over 252 million people globally required humanitarian assistance, while response plans sought USD 33.66 billion but received only USD 8.21 billion.OCHA reported that 326 humanitarian workers were killed across 21 countries in 2025, taking the three-year death toll beyond 1,000.

20.24  World Photography Day 2026 – August 19p.312
Exam Hints:
  • What Event: World Photography Day (WPD) 2026
  • Date: 19 August (Annually)
  • 2026 Theme: “HOME”
  • World Photography Week 2026: 12 August to 26 August 2026
  • First Observation: 19 August 2010
  • First Digital Photo: Captured by Russell Kirsch in 1957
Background:
  • The day commemorates the invention of the daguerreotype process, the first practical photographic process developed by Louis Daguerre in 1837.
2026 Theme:
  • Theme: The theme of World Photography Day 2026 is “HOME”.
Background:
  • Origin: The term “Photography” means “drawing with light” and was coined by British scientist Sir John Herschel in 1839.
  • Inception: The first World Photography Day was observed on 19 August 2010, when nearly 270 photographers shared their photographs in a global online gallery.
  • Week Observance: World Photography Week 2026 will be observed from 12 August to 26 August 2026 as a global celebration encouraging photographers to share meaningful photographs and stories.
Evolution of Photography:
  • Digital Photography: The first digital photograph was captured by Russell Kirsch in 1957, and the first digital still camera was developed by Steven Sasson (Eastman Kodak) in 1975 by British scientist Sir John Herschel in 1839.

20.25  Sadbhavana Divas 2026 – August 20p.313
Exam Hints:
  • What event: Sadbhavana Diwas 2026
  • When: August 20, 2026
  • Significance: Marks the birth anniversary of former PM Rajiv Gandhi
  • First Observed: August 20, 1992
  • Purpose: Promote peace, unity, and communal harmony.
Background:
  • Observation: Sadbhavana Diwas was first observed on August 20, 1992, in memory of Rajiv Gandhi, who was assassinated in 1991.
  • Meaning: The word ‘Sadbhavana’ translates to ‘Goodwill and Bonafide’ in English.
About Rajiv Gandhi:
  • Birth: Rajiv Gandhi was born on August 20, 1944, in Bombay (now Mumbai, Maharashtra).
  • PM: He became the youngest PM of India on October 31, 1984, at the age of 40 years, after the assassination of Indira Gandhi.
  • Constitutional Reform: 61st Constitutional Amendment Act, 1988: During his tenure, this Act lowered the voting age in India from 21 years to 18 years by amending Article 326 of the Constitution.
  • Death: Rajiv Gandhi was assassinated on May 21, 1991, at Sriperumbudur, Tamil Nadu (TN), and his death anniversary is observed as Anti-Terrorism Day in India.
Rajiv Gandhi National Sadbhavana Award:
  • Award Details: Recipients receive a citation and a cash prize of Rs. 10 Lakhs.
  • 2025 Awardee: Salman Khurshid, former Union Minister and senior Congress leader, was honoured with the Rajiv Gandhi National Sadbhavana Award 2025.

20.26  World Mosquito Day 2026 – August 20p.314
Exam Hints:
  • What: World Mosquito Day 2026
  • When: August 20, 2026
  • Objective: Raise awareness on mosquito-borne diseases
  • Commemoration: Discovery of female Anopheles mosquitoes transmit malaria between humans by Sir Ronald Ross in 1897
  • Nobel Prize: Received the Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine in 1902.
Discovery of Malaria Transmission:
  • Malaria Discovery: On 20 August 1897, Sir Ronald Ross discovered malaria parasites in the stomach of female Anopheles mosquitoes in Secunderabad, (Telangana) India.
  • Research Publication: His findings were published in the British Medical Journal (BMJ) on 18 December 1897 in the paper “On some peculiar pigmented cells found in two mosquitoes fed on malarial blood.”
About Sir Ronald Ross:
  • Born: 13 May 1857, Almora, India (present-day Uttarakhand).
  • Honours: Fellow of the Royal College of Surgeons (1901), Fellow of the Royal Society of London, Vice President (1911–1913), and received knighthood in 1911.
Major Vectors:
  • India’s Efforts: National Centre for Vector Borne Diseases Control (NCVBDC) and National Vector Borne Diseases Control Programme (NVBDCP) coordinate prevention measures, with the National Framework for Malaria Elimination (2016) and National Strategic Plan (2023–2027) targeting malaria elimination by 2030.
World Malaria Day:
  • Theme: The 2026 theme is “Driven to End Malaria: Now we Can.

  • When: 21 August, 2026
  • Purpose: Honour & recognise contributions of senior citizens
  • Initiated by: Ronald Reagan, the then former President of USA
  • Related Observance: October 1 as International Day of Older Persons (UN).

Ageing Population Outlook:

  • In India: By 2050, India is projected to have over 300 million citizens aged 60 and above, accounting for nearly one in five Indians.

Background:

20.31  International Day for the Remembrance of the Slave Trade and its Abolition 2026 – August 23p.319
Exam Hints:
  • What: International Day for the Remembrance of the Slave Trade and its Abolition 2026
  • When: 23 August, 2026
  • Aim: Remember victims of slave trade and promote awareness about its abolition
  • Established by: UNESCO (1997 resolution)
  • First Observed: Haiti (23 August 1998)
  • Historical Event: Saint-Domingue uprising (1791).
Background:
  • Proclamation: 29th UNESCO General Conference (1997) adopted Resolution 29 C/40, declaring 23 August as the International Day for the Remembrance of the Slave Trade and its Abolition.
  • History: The day commemorates the uprising that began on the night of 22–23 August 1791 in Saint-Domingue (present-day Haiti), which became a turning point in the Haitian Revolution and contributed to the abolition of the transatlantic slave trade.
  • Observance: The day was first observed in Haiti on 23 August 1998 and later commemorated at Gorée Island, Senegal, in 1999.
  • Led by: The observance is led by the United Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organization (UNESCO).
Haitian Revolution:
  • Beginning: Haitian Revolution (1791–1804) began with the Saint-Domingue uprising on 22–23 August 1791 against French colonial rule.
  • Outcome: Haiti gained independence on 1 January 1804, becoming the first independent Black republic and the only successful slave revolt that established a nation.
UNESCO Slave Route Project:
  • Established: 1945

20.33  World Sanskrit Day 2026 – August 28p.322
Exam Hints:
  • What Day: World Sanskrit Day 2026 (Vishva Samskrita Dinam)
  • Date: 28 August 2026
  • Purpose: Promotion of Sanskrit language and heritage
  • First Observed: 1969
  • Associated Week: Sanskrit Week (24–30 August 2026)
  • Classical Language Status: Declared in 2005.
Background:
  • World Sanskrit Day 2026 was observed on 28 August 2026.
  • World Sanskrit Day 2025 was observed on 9 August 2025, while World Sanskrit Day 2027 will be observed on 17 August 2027.
Background:
  • Origin: In 1969, the Ministry of Education (MoE) initiated the observance of Sanskrit Diwas at central and state levels.
  • Sanskrit Week: Sanskrit Week 2026 is observed from 24 to 30 August 2026 to promote awareness and learning of Sanskrit.
About Sanskrit Language:
  • Overview: Sanskrit is one of the world’s oldest languages and is known as the ‘Mother of All Languages’ and ‘Language of the Gods.’
  • Classical Status: In 2005, the Government of India (GoI) declared Sanskrit as a Classical Language of India and included it in the Eighth Schedule of the Constitution.
  • Official Language Status: Uttarakhand became the first Indian state in 2010 to grant Sanskrit official language status, followed by Himachal Pradesh (HP) as the second state in 2019.

IMPORTANT DAYS
20.34  International Day Against Nuclear Tests 2026 – August 29p.322
Exam Hints:
  • What: International Day Against Nuclear Tests 2026
  • When: 29 August ,2026
  • 2026 Observance: 17th
  • Organiser: UN
  • First Observance: 29 August 2010
  • Significance: Closure of Semipalatinsk Nuclear Test Site, Kazakhstan (1991).
Background:
  • 29 August 2026 marks the 17th observance of IDANT.
Background:
  • Resolution: On 2 December 2009, the 64th United Nations General Assembly (UNGA) adopted Resolution A/RES/64/35, declaring 29 August as the International Day Against Nuclear Tests.
  • First Observance: First observed on 29 August 2010.
  • Significance of Date: Marks the closure of the Semipalatinsk Nuclear Test Site (SNTS) in Kazakhstan on 29 August 1991, where 456 nuclear tests were conducted between 1949–1989.
About World’s First Nuclear Test:
  • Anniversary: The year 2026 marked the 81st anniversary of the world’s first nuclear test.
India’s Nuclear Test & Early Initiatives:
  • Initiatives: In 1954, Jawaharlal Nehru called for a nuclear test ban, followed by the Partial Test Ban Treaty (PTBT), 1963, which prohibited tests in the atmosphere, outer space, and underwater.
About CTBT & Global Context:
  • Treaty Status: The treaty has 187 signatories and 178 ratifications, but is yet to enter into force; India has not signed the treaty.
About United Nations (UN):
  • Secretary-General: António Guterres Headquarters-New York City, the United States of America (USA) Established-1945

22.1  New Catfish Species ‘Pseudolaguvia khairii’ Discovered in Odishap.325
Exam Hints:
  • What: Discovery of a new catfish species
  • Name: Pseudolaguvia khairii
  • Discovered From: Budhabalanga River, Odisha
  • Published In: Zootaxa
  • Taxonomic Order: Siluriformes
  • Named After: Khairi, the legendary tigress of Similipal Tiger Reserve.
Background:
  • The discovery published in the International taxonomic journal Zootaxa.
About Pseudolaguvia khairii:
  • Taxonomy: It belongs to the order Siluriformes, which comprises all species of catfish.
  • Habitat: Pseudolaguvia is a genus of small hillstream catfishes found in fast-flowing rivers across India, Bangladesh, Nepal, and Myanmar.
  • Features: small-sized catfish measuring only a few centimetres in length and is distinguished by a unique fleshy protuberance at the base of its dorsal fin.
  • Significance: The discovery highlights the rich and lesser-known biodiversity of India’s freshwater ecosystems and emphasises the importance of conserving species across all categories, from iconic wildlife to unique aquatic species.
  • About Khairi: orphaned cub rescued from the forests of STR, in the 1970s and was raised by Saroj Raj Choudhury and his wife Nihar Nalini Swain.

22.2  ZSI Completed India’s 1st Catalogue of Lepidoptera: India Hosts 13,703 species of lepidoptera (Butterflies and Moths)p.326
Exam Hints:
  • What: Completion of India’s 1st detailed Catalogue of Lepidoptera (Butterflies and Moths)
  • Compiled by: ZSI under MoEFCC
  • Total Volumes Compiled: 22
  • Catalogue Spearheaded by: Dr. Rahul Joshi and Dr. Navneet Singh (ZSI taxonomists)
  • Published in: Zootaxa
  • Total Number of Lepidoptera in India: 13,703 (including 1,417 butterflies and 12,286 moths)
  • Genera and Families: 3,705 genera, 240 subfamilies, 102 families, and 31 superfamilies
  • Nocturnal Pollinators: Geometroidea (2,205 species), about 8.80% of the world’s known diversity
Key Findings:
  • Total Species Identified: As per ZSI, India hosts 13,703 species of butterflies and moths across 3,705 genera, 240 subfamilies, 102 families, and 31 superfamilies, representing nearly 8.25% of all known butterfly and moth species worldwide.Of the total lepidoptera species identified,1,417 species are butterflies and the remaining 12,286 are moths.
  • Family Composition: Geometridae with 2,100 species in 445 genera, is the largest among the 4 families; followed by Uranidae (95 species in 27 genera); Epicopeiidae (9 species in 6 genera); and Pseudobistonidae (1 species in 1 genus), while Sematuridae family is absent from India.
  • Speciose Subfamilies: Ennominae with 895 species in 189 genera, dominate the most species-rich subfamilies; followed by Larentiinae (565 species in 128 genera), and Geometrinae (309 species in 82 genera), while the subfamilies Epidesmiinae (Geometridae) and Heraculinae (Pseudobistonidae) are each represented by a single species in India.
Zoological Survey of India (ZSI) - Static GK:
Director: Dr. Dhriti Banerjee
Headquarters: Kolkata, West Bengal (WB)
Established: 1916

22.3  Kerala Researchers Identify New Seahorse Species ‘Hippocampus amandavincentae’p.327
Exam Hints:
  • What: Discovery of a new seahorse species ‘Hippocampus amandavincentae’
  • Discovered by: KUFOS
  • Named After: Marine biologist Amanda Vincent
  • Specimens Collected: Tuticorin Fisheries Harbour, TN and Anchuthengu, Kerala
  • Publisher: Zootaxa.
Background:
  • The findings, published in Zootaxa, were based on specimens collected from Tuticorin Fisheries Harbour, Tamil Nadu (TN), and surveys off Anchuthengu, Kerala.
About Hippocampus amandavincentae:
  • Taxonomy: The study established that the historically recognised ‘Hippocampus histrix’ actually comprises three distinct species, correcting decades of misidentification in museum collections and biodiversity databases.
  • Naming: The species was named after Amanda Vincent, an internationally renowned marine biologist, in recognition of her contributions to marine conservation and seahorse research.
  • Significance: The discovery represents the fifth recorded seahorse species from the Kerala coast and strengthens scientific knowledge of India’s marine biodiversity while supporting more accurate conservation planning.

22.4  Delhi University Scientists Discover 3 New Cascade Frog Species in Northeast Indiap.327
Exam Hints:
  • What: Discovery of 3 New Cascade Frog Species
  • By Whom: Led by Prof. S. D. Biju, University of Delhi
  • Genus: Amolops (Cascade frogs)
  • New Species: Amolops ammachi, Amolops sendenyu, Amolops bella
  • Where: Tripura (Jampui Hills), Nagaland and West Bengal
  • Publisher: Bulletin of the Museum of Comparative Zoology, Harvard University
Background:
  • The study was published in the Bulletin of the Museum of Comparative Zoology, Harvard University, highlighting India’s rich amphibian diversity.
Key Details of the Discovery:
  • Research: The study was based on over 15 years of fieldwork covering more than 300 geographic frog populations using DNA (deoxyribonucleic acid) sequencing and morphology.
  • Naming: Ammachi’s Cascade Frog was discovered in Tripura’s Jampui Hills and named after Padmini Varkey, a Kerala-based social worker and philanthropist who was fondly known as “Ammachi”.Sendenyu Cascade Frog was named after the Sendenyu village, Nagaland where it was discovered.Beautiful Cascade Frog, is named for its striking green body with yellowish-green or reddish-brown mosaic patterns.
  • Habitat: Cascade frogs inhabit fast-flowing forest streams and rocky hill environments, in humid ecosystems of the Eastern Himalayas and Northeast India.
Tripura - Static GK:

22.5  Himalayan Bloom names ‘Impatiens nimspurjae’ to Honours Mountaineer Nirmal Purjap.328
Exam Hints:
  • What: Himalayan plant species named Impatiens nimspurjai
  • Genus: Impatiens
  • Discovered in: Myagdi district, Nepal
  • Known as: Tiuri (local name of Impatiens plants in Nepal)
  • Named After: Nirmal “Nimsdai” Purja
  • Achievement: Climbed all 14 peaks of the world’s 8,000-metre peaks in 2019 in record time of 6 months and 6 days
  • Purja Honours: Member of the Order of the British Empire (MBE) by Queen Elizabeth II in 2018, recognised by the UNEP as a Mountain Advocate
Background:
  • The species was discovered in Myagdi district of Nepal by researchers Bhakta Bahadur Raskoti and Rita Ale.
  • The species was formally described in a 57-page study published in the peer-reviewed journal PLOS ONE and was first encountered in western Myagdi district of Nepal in 2011.
About Impatiens Nimspurjai:
  • Taxonomy: Impatiens Nimspurjai belongs to the genus Impatiens of the Balsaminaceae family, a large group of flowering plants locally known as Tiuri in Nepal.
  • Habitat: The species grows on humid slopes and forest margins at an altitude of approximately 2,800m to 2,900m (metres) above sea level
  • About Nirmal ‘Nimsdai’ Purja: He climbed all 14 peaks of the world’s 8,000-metre peaks in 2019 in record time of 6 months and 6 days and was part of the first winter K2 ascent (2021).
  • Honours: Appointed a Member of the Order of the British Empire (MBE) by Queen Elizabeth II in 2018 and was recognised by the United Nations Environment Programme (UNEP) as a Mountain Advocate.

22.6  CSIR-IHBT Researchers Discover New Sunflower Species ‘Doronicum Sanctum’ in ARp.329
Exam Hints:
  • What: Discovery of new sunflower species
  • Name: ‘Doronicum sanctum’
  • By Whom: CSIR-IHBT
  • Where: Tawang district, AR
  • Publication: Springer Nature
  • Significance: First record of Doronicum genus in Northeast India; highlights Himalayan biodiversity.
Background:
  • The findings were published in an international peer-reviewed journal by Springer Nature.
About Doronicum Sanctum:
  • Record: This marks the first documentation of the Doronicum genus in Northeast India, as earlier records in India were limited to the western Himalayan region.
  • Nomenclature: The species, commonly known as Leopard’s bane, was named sanctum as its type locality lies near the Chumi Gyatse Holy Waterfalls, a site of deep spiritual significance to the local community.
  • Characteristics: The species has hairy seeds (pubescent ray cypselae), a short-lived feathery pappus, dilated flower stalks, flat lance-shaped bracts, and bright yellow flowers with heart-shaped leaves, indicating a rare plant.

22.7  Scientists Discovers First Subterranean Fish ‘Gangaichthys Indonepalicus’ in Indo-Gangetic Basinp.329
Exam Hints:
  • What: First subterranean fish recorded from the Indo-Gangetic Basin
  • Name of Species: Gangaichthys Indonepalicus
  • By Whom: Zoological Survey of India, Thackeray Wildlife Foundation
  • Where: Bihar (India) near India–Nepal border
  • Publisher: ZooKeys journal
Background:
  • The findings were published in the scientific journal ZooKeys by an international team of researchers from India and China.
About Gangaichthys Indonepalicus:
  • Name: The genus name Gangaichthys combines Ganga (the Ganges River) and the Greek ichthys (fish).
  • Features: The species shows adaptations to underground environments, including complete lack of pigmentation, reduced skin-covered eyes, an elongated body, possible absence of ribs.
  • Classification: It belongs to the Chaudhuriidae family (earthworm eels) under the Synbranchiformes order and is considered a new genus and species, pending genetic validation.
  • About ZSI: It was established in 1916, headquartered in Kolkata, West Bengal (WB), functions as India’s apex body for animal taxonomy, wildlife research, and faunal exploration.It operates under the Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change (MoEFCC) and is currently headed by Director Dhriti Banerjee.
